When things had returned again to their usual rut, Master Ambrose came to spend a quiet evening with Master Nathaniel.
They sat for some time in silence puffing at their pipes, and then Master Ambrose said, “Tell me what your theory is about Endymion Leer, Nat. He was a double-dyed villain, all right, I suppose?”
Master Nathaniel did not answer at once, and then he said thoughtfully, “I suppose so. I read the report of his defence, however, and his words seemed to me to ring true. But I think there was some evil lurking in his soul, and everything he touched was contaminated by it, even fairy fruit﻿—even Duke Aubrey.”
“And that spiritual sin he accused himself of﻿ ﻿… what do you suppose it was?”
“I think,” said Master Nathaniel slowly, “he may have mishandled the sacred objects of the Mysteries.”
“What are these sacred objects, Nat?”
Master Nathaniel moved uneasily in his chair, and said, with an embarrassed little laugh, “Life and death, I suppose.” He hated being asked about these sorts of things.
Master Ambrose sat for a few moments pondering, and then he said, “It was curious how in all his attacks on you he defeated his own ends.”
“Yes,” cried Master Nathaniel, with much more animation than he had hitherto shown, “that was really very curious. Everything he did produced exactly the opposite effect he had intended it should.

===Chinese===

或许您曾纳闷，像纳撒尼尔·尚特克利尔老爷这样一个浑身毛病、丝毫不具备英雄气概的凡夫俗子，何以竟被选中扮演如此举足轻重的宏大角色。然而，至高无上的精神天命，往往并非专为最强者或至善之辈所预留。
不过，尽管他被选为奥布里公爵的代理人并被引渡迈入古老秘仪的门槛，他在诸多方面却依然没有脱离昔日那个纳撒尼尔老爷的本来面目——古怪诙谐、稚气未脱，且常常蛮不讲理。而且，我唯恐他亦未曾摆脱忧郁之苦的纠缠。我深怀疑虑：迈入秘仪的门槛究竟能否给人带来真正的幸福。或许最终揭晓的终极奥义是极其苦涩的……抑或，纳撒尼尔老爷至今尚未得见那最终的奥秘。
说来奇怪，新加诸其身的殊荣不仅未曾让他志得意满，反而让他莫名感到羞惭——那感觉宛若他在罹患了某种肉体上的残缺畸形之后，头一遭在挚友们探询的目光注视下战战兢兢穿行而过一般。
---
当一切终于重归往昔的平静轨道之后，安布罗斯老爷登门造访，与纳撒尼尔老爷共度一个宁静的傍晚。
他们默默对坐良久，一口一口吞吐着烟斗里的青烟，随后安布罗斯老爷开口道：“纳特，告诉我你对恩底弥翁·利尔究竟是怎么看的。我想，他终究是个无可救药的大恶棍吧？”
纳撒尼尔老爷没有立刻作答，沉吟片刻后若有所思地说：“我想大概是的。不过我拜读了他的辩护词记录，他的字句在我听来却像发自肺腑的真言。但我认为，他的灵魂深处潜藏着某种邪恶，凡经他手之物皆受其玷污，即便是仙果……乃至奥布里公爵亦未能幸免。”
“那么，他指控自己犯下的那一桩精神罪孽……你以为究竟是什么呢？”
“我想，”纳撒尼尔老爷缓缓说道，“他大概是亵渎滥用了秘仪的圣物。”
“这些圣物究竟指什么，纳特？”
纳撒尼尔老爷在椅子里不安地挪动了一下身子，带着一丝尴尬的轻笑说：“我想大概是生命与死亡吧。”他向来讨厌别人向他打探这类深奥玄妙的玄机。
安布罗斯老爷沉思默想了片刻，随即说道：“说来真奇怪，他在向你发难的所有盘算中，竟无一例外落得事与愿违、搬起石头砸自己脚的下场。”
“是啊，”纳撒尼尔老爷接口道，语气比此前显现出浓郁得多的兴致，“那着实离奇得很。他所做的一切，最终产出的后果恰恰与他的初衷南辕北辙。

===Original===

He feared the Chanticleers, and wanted to be rid of them, so he gets Ranulph off to Fairyland, whence nobody had ever before returned. And he manages to get me so discredited that I have to leave Lud, and he thinks me safely out of the way. But, in reality, he was only bringing about his own downfall. I have to leave Lud, and so I go to the farm, and there I find old Gibberty’s incriminating document. While the fact of Ranulph’s having gone off yonder sends me after him, and that is why, I suppose, I come back as Duke Aubrey’s deputy,” and again he gave an embarrassed laugh; and then added dreamily, “It is useless to try and circumvent the Duke.”
“ ‘He who rides the wind needs must go where his steed carries him,’ ” quoted Master Ambrose.
Master Nathaniel smiled, and for some minutes they puffed at their pipes in silence.
Then Master Nathaniel gave a reminiscent chuckle: “Those were queer months that we lived through, Ambrose!” he cried. “All of us, that’s to say those of us who had parts to play, seemed to be living each others’ dreams or dreaming each others’ lives, whichever way you choose to put it, and the most incongruous things began to rhyme﻿—apples and bleeding corpses and trees and ghosts. Yes, all our dreams got entangled. Leer makes a speech about men and trees, and I find the solution of the situation under a herm, which is half a man and half a tree, and you see the juice of fairy fruit and think that it is the dead bleeding﻿—and so on. Yes, my adventures went on getting more and more like a dream till﻿ ﻿… the climax,” and he paused abruptly.
A long silence followed, broken at last by Master Ambrose. “Well, Nat,” he said, “I think I’ve had a lesson in humility. I used to have as good an opinion of myself as most men, I think, but now I’ve learned that I’m a very ordinary sort of fellow, made of very inferior clay to you and my Moonlove﻿—all the things that you know at first hand and I can only take on faith.”
“Suppose, Ambrose, that what we know at first hand is only this﻿—that there is nothing to know?” said Master Nathaniel a little sadly. Then he sank into a brown study, and Master Ambrose, thinking he wanted to be alone, stole quietly from the room.
Master Nathaniel sat gazing moodily into the fire; and his pipe went out without his noticing it. Then the door opened softly, and someone stole in and stood behind his chair.

===Chinese===

他畏惧尚特克利尔家，一心想除掉我们，于是设法把拉努尔夫弄去了仙境之地——那可是一个自古以来从未有人能生还归来的去处。他又千方百计败坏我的名声，逼得我不得不离开路德，自以为终于把我彻底排挤扫除了。然而在现实中，他这不过是在一步步为他自身的覆灭自掘坟墓。我被迫离开路德，于是去了农场，在那里我顺理成章找到了老吉伯蒂那份定罪的铁证文书。而拉努尔夫逃往彼方的事实，又驱使我一路循迹追踪，我想这大概正是我最终作为奥布里公爵的代理人荣归故里的缘由吧，”说着，他又发出一声难为情的轻笑；随即带着几分恍惚的遐思补充道，“妄图与公爵斗心眼、耍手腕，终究是徒劳无功的。”
“‘御风而行者，必随其坐骑驰骋之势而往，’”安布罗斯老爷引经据典道。
纳撒尼尔老爷微微一笑，两人再次陷入沉默，只顾一口接一口抽着烟斗。
随后，纳撒尼尔老爷若有所思地轻声笑了起来：“安布罗斯，咱们一起熬过来的那几个月可真够光怪陆离的！”他感慨道，“咱们所有人，我是说咱们这些在戏里粉墨登场的人，仿佛都在经历彼此的梦境，或者在梦着彼此的现实，随便你怎么说都行；最风马牛不相及的事物竟开始奇妙地互为押韵——苹果与淌血的尸体、树木与幽灵。是的，咱们所有的梦境全纠缠交织在了一起。利尔发表了一通关于人与树的演说，而我却在半人半树的界标神雕像下找到了破局的答案；你瞧见仙果的汁水，便以为那是亡者的鲜血在流淌——诸如此类，不胜枚举。是的，我的种种经历变得越来越像一场大梦，直到……高潮降临，”说到此处，他冷不防戛然而止。
随后是一阵悠长的静默，最终由安布罗斯老爷打破。“好啦，纳特，”他说，“我想我算是上了一堂学会谦卑的大课。以往我总自命不凡，以为自己眼界过人，如今我才醒悟，自己不过是个最平庸无奇的家伙，同你以及我的穆恩拉夫相比，我不过是用最劣等的泥土捏造出来的——所有那些你们能亲身参透体悟的玄机，我却只能凭着信念去盲目信从。”
“安布罗斯，倘若我们亲身体悟到的全部真理，仅仅在于‘世间本就空无一物、无可参透’呢？”纳撒尼尔老爷略带忧伤地幽幽说道。随即他陷入了深沉的沉思之中，安布罗斯老爷以为他想独处，便轻手轻脚地悄然退出了房间。
纳撒尼尔老爷独自坐在壁炉前，神情落寞地凝视着炉火；烟斗熄灭了他也浑然不觉。这时，房门被轻轻推开，一个人悄悄溜了进来，静静立在他的椅背身后。

===Original===

It was Dame Marigold. All she said was, “Funny old Nat!” but her voice had a husky tenderness. And then she knelt down beside him and took him into her soft warm arms. And a new hope was borne in upon Master Nathaniel that someday he would hear the Note again, and all would be clear.

===Chinese===

那是玛丽戈尔德夫人。她吐出的唯有一句轻声昵语：“古怪的老纳特！”然而她的嗓音里却浸润着沙哑的深情。接着，她在他身旁屈膝跪下，将他紧紧揽入温软丰润的臂弯之中。纳撒尼尔老爷心头蓦地升腾起一股崭新的希冀：总有一天，他定会再度听闻那声音符，届时一切都将豁然开朗、明澈如水。

## XXV The Law Crouches and Springs / XXV 法律伏低跃起
===Original===

Literally, Master Polydore Vigil received the severest shock of his life, when a few days after the events recorded in the last chapter there reached him the warrant against Endymion Leer, duly signed and sealed by the lawman of the district of Swan-on-the-Dapple.
Dame Marigold had been right in saying that her brother was now completely under the dominion of the doctor. Master Polydore was a weak, idle man, who, nevertheless, dearly loved the insignia of authority. Hence, his present position was for him an ideal one﻿—he had all the glory due to the first citizen, who has, moreover, effected a coup d’état, and none of the real responsibility that such a situation entails.
And now, this terrible document had arrived﻿—it was like an attempt to cut off his right hand. His first instinct on receiving it was to rush off and take counsel with Endymion Leer himself﻿—surely the omniscient resourceful doctor would be able to reduce to wind and thistledown even a thing as solid as a warrant. But respect for the Law, and the belief that though everything else may turn out vanity and delusion, the Law has the terrifying solidity of Reality itself, were deep-rooted in Master Polydore. If there was a warrant out against Endymion Leer﻿—well, then, he must bend his neck to the yoke like any other citizen and stand his trial.
Again he read through the warrant, in the hopes that on a second it would lose its reality﻿—prove to be a forgery, or a hoax. Alas! Its genuineness was but too unmistakable﻿—the Law had spoken.
Master Polydore let his hands fall to his sides in an attitude of limp dismay; then he sighed heavily; then he rose slowly to his feet﻿—there was nothing for it but to summon Mumchance, and let the warrant instantly be put into effect. As it was possible, nay, almost certain, that the Doctor would be able to clear himself triumphantly in Court, the quicker the business was put through, the sooner Master Polydore would recover his right hand.
When Mumchance arrived, Master Polydore said, in a voice as casual as he could make it, “Oh! yes, Mumchance, yes﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

确切地说，波吕多洛·维吉尔老爷遭遇了他平生最剧烈的一次晴天霹雳：上一章所记述的诸般事端过去数日之后，由达普尔河畔天鹅镇地区执法官正式签署盖印的针对恩底弥翁·利尔的拘捕令，送达了他的案头。
玛丽戈尔德夫人先前说得没错，她的这位胞兄眼下已彻底被医生玩弄于股掌之间。波吕多洛老爷是个懦弱懒散的庸碌之辈，然而骨子里却对权柄的荣耀徽记嗜之如命。因此，他眼下的处境对他而言堪称无可挑剔——他坐拥首位体面市民所应享有的赫赫虚荣，更何况此人还刚刚策动了一场成功的政变，却无需承担任何此等权位所必然招致的实质性重责大任。
然而此刻，这纸骇人听闻的文书却冷不防递到了跟前——那感觉宛若有人要当场生生斩断他的右臂。接到拘票的那一刻，他的第一本能便是急匆匆奔去同恩底弥翁·利尔本人商议对策——料想这位无所不知、智计百出的名医定能翻云覆雨，哪怕是拘票这等铁板钉钉的硬物，也能教他在谈笑间化为轻烟与蓟花飞絮。然而，对法律的敬畏，以及深信世间万物纵然皆属过眼云烟与虚妄梦幻、唯独法律具备真真切切如现实本身般令人毛骨悚然之坚实性的信念，却早已在波吕多洛老爷的心底根深蒂固。倘若真有一纸针对恩底弥翁·利尔的拘捕令签发出来——那么，医生便必须同其他任何寻常市民一样俯首就范，出庭受审。
他反反复复将那份拘票读了又读，寄希望于再看一遍时它会自行瓦解——证明不过是一桩伪造文书或一场恶劣的恶作剧。呜呼！其真实无妄却委实昭然若揭——法律已然开口宣判。
波吕多洛老爷双手颓然垂落身侧，神态满是瘫软绝望；随即他长长叹了一口气；继而缓缓站起身来——别无他法，唯有传唤芒姆钱斯，下令将拘票立即付诸执行。既然医生完全有可能、甚至可以说是几乎必定能在法庭上为自己洗刷冤屈、大获全胜，那么这场公案办得越雷厉风行，波吕多洛老爷就能越快失而复得自己的这条臂膀。
芒姆钱斯应召赶到时，波吕多洛老爷极力用若无其事的漫不经心腔调开口道：“噢！是的，芒姆钱斯，不错……

===Original===

I asked you to come, because,” and he gave a little laugh, “a warrant has actually arrived﻿—of course, there must be some gross misunderstanding behind it, and there will be no difficulty in getting it cleared up in Court﻿—but, as a matter of fact, a warrant has arrived from the lawman of Swan-on-the-Dapple, against﻿ ﻿… well, against none other than Dr. Endymion Leer!” and again he laughed.
“Yes, your Worship,” said Mumchance; and, not only did his face express no surprise, but into the bargain it looked distinctly grim.
“Absurd, isn’t it?” said Master Polydore, “and *most* inconvenient.”
Mumchance cleared his throat: “A murderer’s a murderer, your Worship,” he said. “Me and my wife, we were spending last evening at Mothgreen﻿—my wife’s cousin keeps the tavern there, and he was celebrating his silver wedding﻿—if your Worship will excuse me mentioning such things﻿—and among the friends he’d asked in was the plaintiff and her aunt﻿ ﻿… and, well﻿ ﻿… there be some things that be just too big for any defendant to dodge. But I’ll say no more, your Worship.”
“I should hope not, Mumchance; you have already strangely forgotten yourself,” and Master Polydore glared fiercely at the unrepentant Mumchance. All the same, he could not help feeling a little disquieted by the attitude adopted by that worthy.
Two hours later after a busy morning devoted to professional visits﻿—and, perhaps, some unprofessional ones too﻿—Endymion Leer sat down to his midday dinner. There was not a happier man in Lud than he﻿—he was the most influential man in the town, deep in the counsels of the magistrates; and as for the dreaded Chanticleers﻿—well, he had successively robbed them of their sting. Life being one and indivisible, when one has a sense that it is good its humblest manifestations are transfigured, and that morning the Doctor would have found a meal of baked haws sweet to his palate﻿—how much more so the succulent meal that was actually awaiting him. But it was not fated that Endymion Leer should eat that dinner. There came a loud double knock at the door, and then the voice of Captain Mumchance, demanding instantly to be shown in to the Doctor.

===Chinese===

我唤你前来，是因为，”说着他干笑了一声，“居然当真送来了一份拘捕令——当然，这背后必定存在着某种荒唐绝顶的弥天误会，想必到了公堂之上不费吹灰之力便能冰释澄清——不过，确凿无疑的是，从达普尔河畔天鹅镇执法官那里递送来了一张拘票，逮捕的……咳，不是旁人，正是恩底弥翁·利尔医生！”说着他又是一声干笑。
“遵命，大人，”芒姆钱斯应道；他的脸上不仅没有露出半分讶异之色，而且神情显得异乎寻常的冷峻森严。
“荒唐透顶，不是吗？”波吕多洛老爷说，“而且‘真是’添乱至极。”
芒姆钱斯清了清嗓子：“杀人凶手终究是杀人凶手，大人，”他说，“昨晚我和拙荆在莫斯格林过的夜——我内弟在那儿开酒馆，正逢他庆祝银婚大喜——要是大人不怪小的闲话家常的话——在他请来的亲朋里头，正巧有原告姑娘和她姑妈……咳，有些桩罪孽大得让任何被告都甭想金蝉脱壳。不过小的就不多嘴了，大人。”
“我料你也不敢多嘴，芒姆钱斯；你刚才未免太不知天高地厚了，”波吕多洛老爷恶狠狠地瞪着毫不悔改的芒姆钱斯。尽管如此，瞧着这位队长的强硬派头，他心头还是禁不住掠过一阵忐忑不安。
两小时后（2小时后），在忙碌完一上午的行医问诊——或许其间还夹杂着若干非职业性的密谋拜会——恩底弥翁·利尔坐下来享用他的午餐。在路德城里，恐怕找不出第二个人能比他更心满意足、春风得意了——他是城里最有权势的大红人，在治安官们的重臣密室中深得倚重；至于素来令人忌惮的尚特克利尔家——哼，他早已将其毒牙连根拔除。既然生命浑然一体不可分割，一旦人感到生活无比称心如意，哪怕周遭最卑微的细枝末节也会被镀上一层华彩，那天早晨利尔哪怕面对一盘烤山楂果也会觉得甘之如饴——更遑论眼下恭候着他的这顿珍馐美馔了。然而，恩底弥翁·利尔命中注定吃不上这顿饭了。门外冷不防响起了一阵急促沉重的敲门声，随即传来了芒姆钱斯队长粗声大气的呼喝，勒令立刻面见医生。

===Original===

It was in vain that the housekeeper protested, saying that the Doctor had given strict orders that he was never to be disturbed at his meals, for the Captain roughly brushed her aside with an aphorism worthy of that eminent jurist, the late Master Josiah Chanticleer. “The Law, my good lady, is no respector of a gentleman’s stomach, so I’ll trouble you to stand out of the way,” and he stumped resolutely into the parlour.
“Morning, Mumchance!” cried the Doctor cheerily, “come to share this excellent-looking pigeon-pie?”
For a second or two the Captain surveyed him rather ghoulishly. It must be remembered that not only had the Captain identified himself with the Law to such a degree that he looked upon any breach of it as a personal insult, but that also he had been deeply wounded in his professional pride in that he had not immediately recognised a murderer by his smell.
Captain Mumchance was not exactly an imaginative man, but as he stood there contemplating the Doctor he could almost have believed that his features and expression had suffered a subtle and most unbecoming change since he had last seen them. It was as if he was sitting in a ghastly green light﻿—the most disfiguring and sinister of all the effects of light with which the Law cunningly plays with appearances﻿—the light that emanates from the word *murder*.
“No, thank you,” he said gruffly, “I don’t sit down to table with the likes of you.”
The Doctor gave him a very sharp look, and then he raised his eyebrows and said drily, “It seems to me that recently you have more than once honoured my humble board.”
The Captain snorted, and then in a stentorian and unnatural voice, he shouted, “Endymion Leer! I arrest you in the name of the country of Dorimare, and to the end that the dead, the living, and those not yet born, may rest quietly in their graves, their bed, and the womb.”
“Gammon and spinach!” cried the Doctor, testily, “what’s your little game, Mumchance?”
“Is murder, game?” said the Captain; and at that word the Doctor blanched, and then Mumchance added, “You’re accused of the murder of the late Farmer Gibberty.”
The words acted like a spell. It was as if Endymion Leer’s previous sly, ironical, birdlike personality slipped from him like a mask, revealing another soul, at once more formidable and more tragic.

===Chinese===

女管家极力阻拦抗议，声称医生严令吩咐进餐时绝不容任何人打扰，然而队长却粗暴地将她推到一旁，嘴里吐出一句堪与法学界泰斗、已故约西亚·尚特克利尔老爷相媲美的至理名言：“我好心的妇人，法律可从不讲究体面绅士的肚皮，所以劳驾你给老子让开道来，”说着，他迈着沉重坚定的步伐大踏步闯进了客厅。
“早啊，芒姆钱斯！”医生快活地打着招呼，“是来同我分尝这盘看起来鲜美无比的鸽肉馅饼的吗？”
有那么一两秒钟，队长用一种近乎审视死尸般的阴冷眼神端详着他。须牢记的是，队长不仅早已将自身与法律水乳交融到了将任何违法行径皆视为对自己人格侮辱的境地，而且身为捕头，他此前竟然未能从气味上第一眼辨识出杀人凶手，这令他的职业尊严蒙受了莫大的奇耻大辱。
芒姆钱斯队长绝非一个耽于幻想的文弱之人，但当他伫立在那里死死盯视着医生时，他几乎敢发誓对方的面容与神态在不知不觉间已悄然发生了一种极其隐秘而狰狞的蜕变。那感觉宛若他正沐浴在一道阴森可怖的惨绿幽光之中——那是法律在操弄表象时所催生出的最能令人面目全非、最阴冷不祥的幽光——这道光芒源自那个字眼：谋杀。
“不必了，多谢，”他生硬粗暴地说，“老子从不跟你们这号人同桌进餐。”
医生飞快地睃了他一眼，随即扬起眉毛，冷冷地说：“据我所知，近来你可不止一次赏光屈尊寒舍的小宴了。”
队长从鼻子里重重哼了一声，接着用洪亮雷鸣却异样失真的声调厉声断喝道：“恩底弥翁·利尔！我以多里梅尔共和国的名义将你逮捕归案，好教亡者安眠于墓穴，生者安歇于床榻，未出世之婴孩安宁于母腹！”
“简直胡言乱语！”医生烦躁地斥责道，“芒姆钱斯，你这是在玩什么花招？”
“谋杀，能算是花招吗？”队长冷冷反诘；听到那个词，医生的脸色刷地褪尽了血色，芒姆钱斯随即补充道，“你被控谋杀已故农夫吉伯蒂。”
这番话宛如一记不可思议的魔咒。仿佛恩底弥翁·利尔往昔那副狡黠、冷嘲热讽、宛如飞禽般敏锐的伪装，如面具般从他脸上剥落，暴露出另一副截然不同、更为凶悍且更为悲怆的灵魂。

===Original===

For a few seconds he stood white and silent, and then he cried out in a terrible voice: “Treachery! Treachery! The Silent People have betrayed me! It is ill serving a perfidious master!”
---
The news of the arrest of Endymion Leer on a charge of murder flew like wildfire through Lud.
At all the street corners, little groups of tradesmen, ’prentices, sailors, were to be seen engaged in excited conversation, and from one to the other group flitted the deaf-mute harlot, Bawdy Bess, inciting them in her strange uncontrolled speech, while dogging her footsteps with her dance-like tread went old Mother Tibbs, alternately laughing in crazy glee and weeping and wringing her hands and crying out that she had not yet brought back the Doctor’s last washing, and it was a sad thing that he should go for his last ride in foul linen. “For he’ll mount Duke Aubrey’s wooden horse﻿—the Gentlemen have told me so,” she added with mysterious nods.
---
In the meantime, Luke Hempen had reported to Mumchance what he had learned from the little herdsmen about the “fish” caught by the widow and the Doctor. The Yeomanry stationed on the border were instantly notified and ordered to drag the Dapple near the spot where it bubbled out after its subterranean passage through the Debatable Hills. They did so, and discovered wicker frails of fairy fruit, so cunningly weighted that they were able to float under the surface of the water.
This discovery considerably altered Master Polydore’s attitude to Endymion Leer.

===Chinese===

有那么几秒钟，他面色惨白、凝固般伫立无声，随即用一声撕心裂肺的可怖嗓音尖叫道：“背叛！滔天的背叛！沉默之民出卖了我！侍奉奸佞狡诈的主子，终究没有好下场啊！”
---
恩底弥翁·利尔因谋杀罪被捕的惊天巨变，宛若荒原野火般在路德城中疯狂蔓延。
在每一个街角，都能见到小商贩、学徒、水手们三五成群，群情激愤地交头接耳，而在各个人群之间穿梭飞掠的，正是那个聋哑暗娼浪荡贝丝，用她那怪诞失控的粗哑嗓音挑动煽惑着众人；紧随其后迈着碎步起舞的，则是疯颠的老蒂布斯大妈，她一会儿疯疯癫癫地狂笑，一会儿又痛哭流涕、捶胸顿足，哭嚷着自己还没把医生送洗的衣物送还回去，要让他穿着脏衣服踏上最后的归宿，那可真是太造孽了。“因为他要骑上奥布里公爵的木马了——那些老爷们已经把这消息透露给我啦，”她神秘兮兮地连连点头补充道。
---
与此同时，卢克·亨彭已向芒姆钱斯如实禀报了他从小牧童口中打探到的关于寡妇与医生捕获的所谓“鱼”的真相。驻守边陲的义勇卫队即刻接获通报，奉命前往达普尔河潜流穿透争议山丘喷涌而出的泉眼近旁进行打捞拉网。他们依令行事，果真捞起了一筐筐用柳条编制的扁篓，里面紧密塞满了仙果，其配重设计精妙绝伦，恰好能悬浮在水面之下顺流漂移。
这一铁证的查获，彻底扭转了波吕多洛老爷对恩底弥翁·利尔的态度。

## XXIII The Northern Firebox and Dead Men’s Tales / XXIII 北方火箱与死人的传说
===Original===

That night Hazel could not get to sleep. Perhaps this was due to having noticed something that afternoon that made her vaguely uneasy. The evenings were beginning to be chilly, and, shortly before supper, she had gone up to Master Nathaniel’s room to light his fire. She found the widow and one of the maidservants there before her, and, to her surprise, they had brought down from the attic an old charcoal stove that had lain there unused for years, for Dorimare was a land of open fires, and stoves were practically unknown. The widow had brought the stove to the farm on her marriage, for, on her mother’s side, she had belonged to a race from the far North.
On Hazel’s look of surprise, she had said casually, “The logs are dampish today, and I thought this would make our guest cozier.”
Now Hazel knew that the wood was not in the least damp; how could it be, as it had not rained for days? But that this should have made her uneasy was a sign of her deep instinctive distrust of her grandfather’s widow.
Perhaps the strongest instinct in Hazel was that of hospitality﻿—that all should be well, physically and morally, with the guests under the roof that she never forgot was hers, was a need in her much more pressing than any welfare of her own.
Meanwhile, Master Nathaniel, somewhat puzzled by the outlandish apparatus that was warming his room, had got into bed. He did not immediately put out his candle; he wished to think. For being much given to reverie, when he wanted to follow the sterner path of consecutive thought, he liked to have some tangible object on which to focus his eye, a visible goal, as it were, to keep his feet from straying down the shadowy paths that he so much preferred.
Tonight it was the fine embossed ceiling on which he fixed his eye﻿—the same ceiling at which Ranulph used to gaze when he had slept in this room. On a ground of a rich claret colour patterned with azure arabesques, knobs of a dull gold were embossed, and at the four corners clustered bunches of grapes and scarlet berries in stucco. And though time had dulled their colour and robbed the clusters of many of their berries, they remained, nevertheless, pretty and realistic objects.
But, in spite of the light, the focus, and his desire for hard thinking, Master Nathaniel found his thoughts drifting down the most fantastic paths.

===Chinese===

那一夜，黑兹尔翻来覆去无法入眠。这或许是因为她那天下午察觉到的异样，令她心头隐隐掠过一阵不安。近来入夜渐微凉，就在晚饭前不久，她曾上楼到纳撒尼尔老爷房里帮他生火。却不料寡妇和一个女仆早已先她一步在那里了，而且令她惊讶的是，她们居然从阁楼里搬下来了一具闲置多年的老旧木炭火炉——须知多里梅尔素来习惯于明火壁炉取暖，这种火炉在本地几乎闻所未闻。这具火炉是寡妇当年出嫁时带到农场来的，因其母系一脉乃是来自遥远极北之地的族裔。
见黑兹尔一脸诧异，寡妇当时漫不经心地随口敷衍道：“今天的柴火有些发潮，我想用这个能教咱们的贵客暖和舒适些。”
然而黑兹尔心里清楚，那些劈柴根本没有半分受潮；接连好几天未曾落过一滴雨，柴火又怎会发潮呢？但此事竟令她如此惴惴不安，正足见她出于本能对祖父这位续弦寡妇深藏着何等深沉的戒备与猜忌。
在黑兹尔的秉性之中，或许最为根深蒂固的天性莫过于好客——使置身于这个她时刻牢记属于自家屋檐下的宾客在身心两方面皆安然妥帖，在她看来是一桩远比她自身任何福祉更为紧迫切实的要务。
与此同时，纳撒尼尔老爷对于这具用来为房间取暖的异邦古怪器具虽心生疑惑，却也已宽衣上床。他并未立刻吹熄蜡烛，他想要静静思索一番。盖因他生性酷爱沉溺遐思，每当他想要强打精神遵循更严谨的连贯逻辑进行理性思考时，总习惯在眼前寻觅某件看得见摸得着的具体实物作为视觉焦点——宛若一个看得见的锚点，以防自己的双脚不由自主误入那些他素来偏爱的朦胧幽径。
今夜，他凝神盯视的焦点正是头顶那面华丽的浮雕天花板——当年拉努尔夫睡在这间房里时，也曾久久凝视着这一面天花板。在深浓如红葡萄酒般的底色上，勾勒着天蓝色的卷草缠枝纹，凸起镶嵌着一颗颗黯淡的金色圆纽，四角则用石膏堆塑出成簇的葡萄串与猩红的浆果。尽管岁月早已剥蚀了它们的明艳光彩、扯落了果串上的诸多浆果，但它们看起来依然精巧别致、栩栩如生。
然而，纵然有烛火的照明、视线的聚焦，以及他渴望缜密深思的意志，纳撒尼尔老爷却发现自己的思绪依旧无可挽回地滑向了最荒诞离奇的幽径。

===Original===

And, besides, he was so drowsy and his limbs felt so strangely heavy. The colours on the ceiling were getting all blurred, and the old knobs were detaching themselves from their background and shining in space like suns, moons, and stars﻿—or was it like apples﻿—the golden apples of the West? And now the claret-coloured background was turning into a red field﻿—a field of red flowers, from which leered Portunus, and among which wept Ranulph. But the straight road, which for the last few months had been the projection of his unknown, buried purpose, even through this confused landscape glimmered white﻿ ﻿… yet, it looked different from usual﻿ ﻿… why, of course, it was the Milky Way! And then he knew no more.
In the meantime Hazel had been growing more and more restless, and, though she scolded herself for foolishness, more and more anxious. Finally, she could stand it no more: “I think I’ll just creep up to the gentleman’s door and listen if I can hear him snoring,” she said to herself. Hazel believed that it was a masculine peculiarity not to be able to sleep without snoring.
But though she kept her ear to the keyhole for a full two minutes, not a sound proceeded from Master Nathaniel’s room. Then she softly opened the door. A lighted candle was guttering to its end, and her guest was lying, to all appearance, dead, whilst a suffocating atmosphere pervaded the room. Hazel felt almost sick with terror, but she flung open the casement window as wide as it would go, poured half the water from the ewer into the stove to extinguish its fire, and the remainder over Master Nathaniel himself. To her unspeakable relief he opened his eyes, groaned, and muttered something inaudible.
“Oh, sir, you’re not dead then!” almost sobbed Hazel. “I’ll just go and fetch you a cup of cordial and get you some hartshorn.”
When she returned with the two restoratives, she found Master Nathaniel sitting up in bed, and, though he looked a little fuddled, his natural colour was creeping back, and the cordial restored him to almost his normal condition.
When Hazel saw that he was really himself again, she sank down on the floor and, spent with terror, began to sob bitterly.
“Come, my child!” said Master Nathaniel kindly, “there’s nothing to cry about. I’m feeling as well as ever I did in my life﻿ ﻿… though, by the Harvest of Souls, I can’t imagine what can have taken me.

===Chinese===

况且，他只觉一阵阵倦意排山倒海般袭来，四肢显得异乎寻常的沉重。天花板上的色彩开始融作一片模糊，那些古旧的圆纽仿佛脱离了背景，在虚空中璀璨闪耀宛若日月星辰——抑或是像苹果……西方的金苹果？而此刻那深红如酒的底色正化作一片猩红的原野——一片盛开着红花的花原，波尔图努斯从花丛间投来斜睨冷笑，拉努尔夫则在其中哀哀抽泣。然而那条笔直的大道——在过去几个月里一直是他内心深处未知隐秘宿愿的具象投射——即便穿过这片纷乱迷离的景象，依然泛着莹莹白光……然而，它看起来却与往常大不相同……哎呀，当然了，那分明就是银河！随即，他便彻底人事不知了。
与此同时，黑兹尔在自己的房里却变得愈发坐立难安，尽管她暗自责备自己神经过敏、胡思乱想，心头的忧虑却如潮水般不可遏制地疯长。最终，她再也无法按捺下去了：“我想我还是悄悄摸到那位老爷门外听听动静吧，瞧能不能听见他的鼾声，”她自言自语道。在黑兹尔的认知里，男人睡觉若是不打鼾，简直就违背了常理。
然而，尽管她把耳朵紧紧贴在锁孔上足足听了整整两分钟（2分钟），纳撒尼尔老爷的房里却没有传出半点声息。于是她轻轻推开了房门。一支点燃的蜡烛正滴落着残蜡行将燃尽，而她的贵客仰面躺在床上，俨然已气绝身亡，满屋子弥漫着令人窒息的浓重浊气。黑兹尔吓得几乎要瘫软过去，但她猛地将双扇玻璃窗推开到最大程度，将水罐里的大半罐冷水哗啦浇进火炉熄灭炭火，剩余的冷水则劈头盖脸全泼在纳撒尼尔老爷身上。令她莫大宽慰的是，老爷缓缓睁开了双眼，痛苦地呻吟了一声，嘴里含糊不清地嘟囔着什么。
“噢，老爷，您原来没死啊！”黑兹尔几乎带着哭腔喊道，“我这就去给您端一杯强心甘露，再拿些鹿角精来给您提神。”
当她端着两样急救之物折返回来时，纳撒尼尔老爷已经在床上坐起身来，尽管神色看起来仍有些浑噩发懵，但他原本的红润气色已渐渐回泛，服下甘露后，他的精气神几乎完全恢复了常态。
眼见他当真安然无恙，黑兹尔脱力般瘫坐在地毯上，劫后余生的惊恐令她伤心地痛哭失声。
“好啦，好孩子！”纳撒尼尔老爷温和地抚慰道，“没什么好哭的了。我感觉自己身子骨好得很，平生从没这么爽利过……不过，凭灵魂之秋起誓，我真纳闷自己刚才究竟中什么邪了。

===Original===

I never remember to have swooned before in all my born days.”
But Hazel would not be comforted: “That it should have happened, here, in my house,” she sobbed. “We who have always stood by the laws of hospitality﻿ ﻿… and not a *young* gentleman, either﻿ ﻿… oh, dearie me; oh, dearie me!”
“What do you blame to yourself, my child?” asked Master Nathaniel. “Your hospitality is in no sense to blame if, owing perhaps to recent fatigues and anxieties, I should have turned faint. No, it is not you that are the bad host, but I that am the bad guest to have given so much trouble.”
But Hazel’s sobs only grew wilder. “I didn’t like her bringing in that firebox﻿—no I didn’t! An evil outlandish thing that it is! That it should have happened under *my* roof! For it *is* my roof﻿ ﻿… and *she’ll* not pass another night under it!” and she sprang to her feet, with clenched fists and blazing eyes.
Master Nathaniel was becoming interested. “Are you alluding to your grandfather’s widow?” he asked quietly.
“Yes, I am!” cried Hazel indignantly. “Oh! she’s up to strange tricks, always﻿ ﻿… and none of her ways are those of honest farmers﻿—no fennel over our doors, unholy fodder in our granary﻿ ﻿… and in her heart, thoughts as unholy. I saw the smile with which she looked at you at dinner.”
“Are you accusing this woman of actually having made an attempt on my life?” he asked slowly.
But Hazel flinched before this point-blank question, and her only answer was to begin again to cry. For a few minutes Master Nathaniel allowed her to do so unmolested, and then he said gently, “I think you have cried enough for tonight, my child. *You* have been kindness itself, but it is evident that I am not very welcome to your grandfather’s widow, so I must not inflict myself longer upon her. But before I leave her roof there is something I want to do, and I shall need your help.”
Then he told her who he was and how he wanted to prove something against a certain enemy of his, and had come here hoping to find a missing clue.
He paused, and looked at her meditatively. “I think I ought to tell you, my child,” he went on, “that if I can prove what I want, your grandmother may also be involved. Did you know she had once been tried for the murder of your grandfather?”
“Yes,” she faltered. “I’ve heard that there was a trial. But I thought she was proved innocent.”
“Yes.

===Chinese===

在我打娘胎里出来的所有日子里，我还从没记得自己曾晕厥过去呢。”
然而黑兹尔却无论如何也无法释怀：“偏偏会发生在这里，在我的家里，”她抽泣着说，“我们家历来最恪守待客之道……而且您又不是什么年轻力壮的少年后生……噢，天哪，天哪，这可怎么得了！”
“你干嘛把罪过往自己身上揽呢，好孩子？”纳撒尼尔老爷问道，“若是因为近来的奔波劳碌与焦虑操心，我自己身子虚脱晕了过去，又怎能归咎于你的款待不周呢？不，错不在你这个热情的主人，全怪我这个笨拙的客人平白添了这许多乱子。”
但黑兹尔的呜咽反而愈发悲切狂乱了。“我打一开始就不乐意她搬进那只火箱——我打心底里就不乐意！那是一件邪门害人的异邦怪物！这等脏心烂肺的事偏偏发生在我家的屋檐下！因为这‘分明’是我的屋檐……而‘她’休想在我的屋顶下再多待哪怕一个晚上！”说着她霍然站起身来，双拳紧攥，双眸燃烧着熊熊怒火。
纳撒尼尔老爷的神色渐渐变得凝重而警觉。“你指的是你祖父的遗孀吗？”他平静地探询道。
“没错，正是她！”黑兹尔义愤填膺地叫道，“噢！她整天都在暗地里捣鬼，尽干些伤天害理的勾当……她所有的行径根本没有半点正派农人的模样——门楣上从不挂茴香，粮仓里堆满了见不得光的诡谲饲料……而她的肚肠里，更藏着同样大逆不道的阴谋。晚饭席间她看您的那副眼神，我全都看在眼里了！”
“你是说，你指控这个妇人方才蓄意图谋害我性命吗？”他缓缓问道。
面对这一开门见山的直白质问，黑兹尔畏缩了一下，唯一的反应便是又低头饮泣起来。纳撒尼尔老爷由着她哭了几分钟未加打扰，随后和颜悦色地说：“我想你今夜流的眼泪已经够多了，好孩子。‘你’待我可谓仁至义尽，但很显然，你祖父的遗孀并不怎么欢迎我的到来，因此我绝不该再厚着脸皮叨扰她了。但在我离开她的屋檐之前，有一桩要紧事我非办不可，而且我需要你的襄助。”
随即他向她坦陈了自己的真实身份，说明自己急需搜集铁证以揭露某个死敌的罪行，正是抱着探寻遗失线索的希冀才专程赶赴此地。
他略作停顿，若有所思地端详着她。“我想我应当如实相告，好孩子，”他继续说道，“倘若我当真能证实我所追查的真相，你的祖母或许亦难逃法网。你可知晓她当年曾因涉嫌谋杀你祖父而受审公堂？”
“我知道，”她怯生生地答道，“我听说过当年曾有过一场审判。但我一直以为她已被证实是清白无辜的了。”
“不错。

===Original===

But there is such a thing as a miscarriage of justice. I believe that your grandfather *was* murdered, and that my enemy﻿—whose name I don’t care to mention till I have more to go upon﻿—had a hand in the matter. And I have a shrewd suspicion that the widow was his accomplice. Under these circumstances, will you still be willing to help me?”
Hazel first turned red, and then she turned white, and her lower lip began to tremble. She disliked the widow, but had to admit that she had never been unkindly treated by her, and, though not her own kith and kin, she was the nearest approach to a relative she could remember. But, on the other hand, Hazel belonged by tradition and breed to the votaries of the grim cult of the Law. Crime must not go unpunished; moreover (and here Hazel subscribed to a still more venerable code) one’s own kith and kin must not go unavenged.
But the very vehemence with which she longed to be rid of the widow’s control had bred a curious irrational sense of guilt with regard to her; and, into the bargain, she was terrified of her.
Supposing this clue should lead to nothing, and the widow discover that they had been imagining? How, in that case, should she dare to face her, to go on living under the same roof with her?
And yet﻿ ﻿… she was certain she had tried to murder their guest that night. How dared she? How dared she?
Hazel clenched her fists, and in a little gasping voice said, “Yes, sir, I’ll help you.”
“Good!” said Master Nathaniel briskly. “I want to take old Portunus’s advice﻿—and dig under that herm in the orchard, this very night. Though, mind you, it’s just as likely as not to prove nothing but the ravings of a crazy mind; or else it may concern some buried treasure, or something else that has nothing to do with your grandfather’s murder. But, in the case of our finding a valuable bit of evidence, we must have witnesses. And I think we should have the lawman of the district with us; who is he?”
“It’s the Swan blacksmith, Peter Pease.”
“Is there any servant you trust whom you could send for him? Someone more attached to you than to the widow?”
“I can trust them all, and they all like me best,” she answered.
“Good. Go and wake a servant and send him off at once for the blacksmith. Tell him not to bring him up to the house, but to take him straight to the orchard﻿ ﻿… we don’t want to wake the widow before need be.

===Chinese===

然而世间确有冤假错案与司法的疏漏。我确信你的祖父‘确实’是被谋杀的，而我的那位仇敌——在掌握更确凿的真凭实据之前我尚不便道出其名号——在其中必定难脱干系。而且我极有把握地怀疑，那位寡妇正是他的同谋共犯。在这一情境之下，你可依然甘愿助我一臂之力吗？”
黑兹尔的脸庞先是涨得通红，继而刷地褪尽血色，下唇开始剧烈地颤抖。她厌恶寡妇，但不得不承认寡妇待她从未有过苛待虐薄，而且尽管并无骨肉血亲之系，寡妇却也是她记忆中唯一的亲近长辈。然而另一方面，依照血脉与家训传统，黑兹尔骨子里是严苛冷酷之法律崇拜的忠诚信徒。罪恶决不可逍遥法外；况且（此处黑兹尔恪守的是一项更为古老神圣的宗法信条），骨肉至亲的血仇决不可沉冤莫雪。
然而，她内心深处渴望摆脱寡妇掌控的那份决绝冲动，反倒在她心头滋生出一种异样而毫无理性的负罪感；更何况，她骨子里对寡妇畏惧到了极点。
万一这条线索最终证明不过是一场空，而寡妇发觉了他们的捕风捉影呢？若真如此，她日后还怎敢面对寡妇，又怎敢继续同她同处一个屋檐之下？
然而……她确信寡妇今晚确曾图谋毒害她们的宾客。她怎敢如此伤天害理？她怎敢如此胆大妄为？
黑兹尔咬牙紧握双拳，用带着微喘的细弱嗓音斩钉截铁地应道：“遵命，老爷，我愿意帮您。”
“好极了！”纳撒尼尔老爷精神大振，“我打算采纳老波尔图努斯的指引——就在今夜，把果园里那尊界标神石柱的底座彻底掘开。尽管你得明白，这极有可能不过是一个疯癫老儿的胡言乱语；或者不过涉及某处埋藏的金银财宝，同你祖父的血案毫无瓜葛。然而，倘若我们当真起获了至关重要的确凿罪证，我们必须有公正的见证人在场。我认为我们应当请本地区的执法官亲临见证；他是何人？”
“是天鹅镇的铁匠彼得·皮斯。”
“你可有信得过的仆从能打发去请他？必须是个忠于你而非偏袒寡妇的可靠之人。”
“府里的雇工我都信得过，而且他们全都向着我，”她回答道。
“很好。去叫醒一名仆人，打发他即刻动身去请铁匠。叮嘱他千万别把铁匠引到宅邸跟前来，直接领到果园里汇合……在非必要之前，咱们可不想惊动了寡妇。

===Original===

And the servant can stay and help us with the job﻿—the more witnesses the better.”
Hazel felt as if she was in a strange, rather terrible dream. But she crept up to the attic and aroused one of the unmarried labourers﻿—who, according to the old custom, slept in their master’s house﻿—and bade him ride into Swan and bring the blacksmith back with him on important business concerning the law.
Hazel calculated that he should get to Swan and back in less than an hour, and she and Master Nathaniel crept out of the house to wait for them in the orchard, each provided with a spade.
The moon was on the wane, but still sufficiently full to give a good light. She was, indeed, an orchard thief, for no fruit being left to rob, she had robbed the leaves of all their colour.
“Poor old moon!” chuckled Master Nathaniel, who was now in the highest of spirits, “always filching colours with which to paint her own pale face, and all in vain! But just look at your friend, at Master Herm. He *does* look knowing!”
For in the moonlight the old herm had found his element, and under her rays his stone flickered and glimmered into living silver flesh, while his archaic smile had gained a new significance.
“Excuse, me, sir,” said Hazel timidly, “but I couldn’t help wondering if the gentleman you suspected was﻿ ﻿… Dr. Leer.”
“What makes you think so?” asked Master Nathaniel sharply.
“I don’t quite know,” faltered Hazel. “I just﻿—wondered.”
Before long they were joined by the labourer and the lawman blacksmith﻿—a burly, jovial, red-haired rustic of about fifty.
“Good evening,” cried Master Nathaniel briskly, “I am Nathaniel Chanticleer” (he was sure that the news of his deposition could not yet have had time to travel to Swan) “and if my business were not very pressing and secret I would not, you may be sure, have had you roused from your bed at this ungodly hour. I have reason to think that something of great importance may be hidden under this herm, and I wanted you to be there to see that our proceedings are all in order,” and he laughed genially. “And here’s the guarantee that I’m no masquerader,” and he removed his signet ring and held it out to the blacksmith. It was engraved with his well-known crest, and with six chevrons, in token that six of his ancestors had been High Seneschals of Dorimare.

===Chinese===

那名雇工也可以留下来搭把手帮咱们干活——见证人自然是越多越妥当。”
黑兹尔只觉自己仿佛置身于一场光怪陆离、阴森恐怖的梦境之中。但她依旧轻手轻脚爬上阁楼，唤醒了一名未婚雇工——依照古老规矩，未婚长工宿在东家的主宅里——吩咐他立刻骑马赶往天鹅镇，把铁匠请来处置一桩关乎法律的紧急要务。
黑兹尔暗自盘算，来回一趟天鹅镇耗时应当不到一小时（1小时）。她与纳撒尼尔老爷各携一把铁锹，悄无声息地溜出了房舍，潜入果园静候接应。
月亮虽已开始亏缺，清辉却依然足以照亮夜色。此时的明月真可谓果园里的窃贼，因为枝头早已没有果实可盗，她便将树叶上所有的翠色洗劫一空。
“可怜的老月亮！”心情大好的纳撒尼尔老爷打趣地轻笑起来，“终日偷取凡间的色彩来涂抹自身惨白的面庞，到头来却全是白费力气！不过快瞧瞧你的那位老朋友——界标神老爷吧。他那副神情可真够高深莫测的！”
在皎洁的月光之下，古老的界标神雕柱仿佛如鱼得水找到了灵魂的归宿，在月华的沐浴下，冰冷的石料忽明忽暗泛着宛如活生生银白肌肤般的微光，那抹古朴深邃的微笑更被赋予了一种全新的神秘意蕴。
“恕小的冒昧，老爷，”黑兹尔怯生生地开口道，“我心里禁不住琢磨，您怀疑的那位大人物……莫非是利尔医生？”
“你因何会有此等揣测？”纳撒尼尔老爷目光如炬、锐利地逼问道。
“我也说不太准，”黑兹尔支支吾吾地说，“我只是……凭空瞎琢磨罢了。”
不多时，雇工引着身为执法官的铁匠赶到了——那是一个年约五十（50岁）上下、身材魁梧健硕、红发红须且生性豪爽的乡下汉子。
“晚上好，”纳撒尼尔老爷开门见山、语气干练地迎上前去，“我是纳撒尼尔·尚特克利尔”（他笃定自己被罢黜的邸报绝无可能如此迅捷传至天鹅镇），“若非干系极其重大且万分隐秘，想必诸位应当明白，我断不会在这等深更半夜扰人清梦。我有充分理由怀疑，在这尊界标神石柱之下深埋着极其重大的物事，特意相邀阁下亲临，以确保咱们今夜的举措完全合乎法度程序，”说着他豪迈地朗声大笑，“喏，这是证明我绝非冒名顶替之徒的铁证凭信，”说着他褪下尾指上的图章戒指递予铁匠过目。戒面上镂刻着他名震遐迩的家族纹章，并缀有六道波状折条纹（6道折条纹），象征着他的家族曾有六位先祖（6位先祖）高居多里梅尔大总管的显赫尊位。

===Original===

Both the blacksmith and the labourer were at first quite overwhelmed by learning his identity, but he pressed a spade into the hand of each and begged them to begin digging without further delay.
For some time they toiled away in silence, and then one of the spades came against something hard.
It proved to be a small iron box with a key attached to it.
“Out with it! out with it!” cried Master Nathaniel excitedly. “I wonder if it contains a halter! By the Sun, Moon and Stars, I wonder!”
But he was sobered by a glimpse of poor Hazel’s scared face.
“Forgive me, my child,” he said gently, “my thirst for revenge has made me forget both decency and manners. And, as like as not, there will be nothing in it but a handful of Duke Aubrey crowns﻿—the nest-egg of one of your ancestors.”
They unlocked the box and found that it contained nothing but a sealed parchment package, addressed thus:

> “To the First Who Finds Me.”

“I think, Miss Hazel, it should be you who opens it. Don’t you agree, Master Lawman?” said Master Nathaniel. So, with trembling fingers, Hazel broke the seal, tore open the wrapper, and drew out a sheet of writing.
By the light of the blacksmith’s lantern they read as follows:

===Chinese===

闻悉眼前之人的赫赫身世，铁匠与雇工起初皆惊愕得手足无措，纳撒尼尔老爷却不由分说往两人手中各塞了一柄铁锹，恳请他们莫要延误时机，即刻破土挖掘。
在一片肃穆的静默之中，众人挥锹苦干良久，忽然间，一柄铁锹铿然撞击在了某种坚硬的器物之上。
掘出一瞧，赫然是一只附着钥匙的精巧铁盒。
“快启出来！快启出来！”纳撒尼尔老爷按捺不住狂喜失声惊呼，“不知这里头可否藏着一根索命的绞索绳！凭太阳、月亮和星辰起誓，老夫委实翘首以盼！”
然而瞥见可怜的黑兹尔那煞白惊骇的脸庞，他立刻收敛了狂态。
“宽恕我吧，好孩子，”他温存地说，“老夫一时被复仇的焦渴蒙蔽了心智，竟将体面修养与待人礼数抛到了九霄云外。而且依我看，里头多半不过是一捧奥布里公爵时期的克朗金币罢了——大概是你家某位先祖私藏的一笔私房底子。”
他们旋开锁簧掀开盒盖，只见铁盒之中唯有一封用火漆严密封口的羊皮纸包裹，其上题字赫然写道：

> 「致首位发现我之人。」

“我想，黑兹尔姑娘，由你来亲手拆启最为公道。执法官老爷，意下如何？”纳撒尼尔老爷提议道。于是，黑兹尔用颤抖的纤指剥去火漆封印，扯开封套，抽出一张写满字迹的信笺。
借着铁匠手中风灯幽微的光亮，众人肃然辨读出如下字句：

===Original===

> I, Jeremiah Gibberty, farmer and lawman of the district of Swan-on-the-Dapple, having ever been a merry man who loved his joke, do herein crack my last, this side of the Debatable Hills, in the hopes that it will not lie so long in the damp earth as to prove but a lame rocket when the time comes to fire it off. And this is my last joke, and may all who hear it hold their sides, and may the tears run down their cheeks. I, Jeremiah Gibberty, was wilfully murdered by my second wife, Clementina, daughter of Ralph Baldbreeches, sailor, and an outlandish woman from the far North. In the which crime she was aided by her lover, Christopher Pugwalker, a foreigner who called himself an herbalist. And I know by sundry itchings of my skin which torment me as I write and by my spotted tongue that I have been given what the folks who know them call death-berries. And they were boiled down to look like mulberry jelly, offered to me by my dear wife, and of which I ate in my innocence. And I bid him who finds this writing to search for a little lad, by name Peter Pease, the son of a tipsy tinker. For this little lad, having an empty stomach and being greedy for pence, came to me but an hour ago with a basket full of these same death-berries and asked me if I should like to buy them. And I, to test him, asked him if he thought there was a blight in my orchard that I should be so hard put to it for fruit. And he said he thought we must like them up at Gibberty’s for he had seen the gentleman who lived with us (Christopher Pugwalker) but a week since, gathering them. And if Christopher Pugwalker should leave these parts, then let the law search for a dumpy fellow, with nut-brown hair, a pug-nose freckled like a robin’s egg, and one eye brown, the other blue. And in order that my last joke may be a well-built one, I have tested the berries I bought from the little lad, though it wrung my heart to do so, on one of the rabbits of a certain little maid, by name, Marjory Beach, the daughter of my carter. And I have done so because she, being seven years old and a healthy lass, runs a good chance of being still this side of the hills when someone digs up this buried jest. And if she be alive she will not have forgotten how one of her rabbits took to scratching itself, and how its tongue was spotted like a snake, and how she found it lying dead.

===Chinese===

> 余，杰里迈亚·吉伯蒂，达普尔河畔天鹅镇地区农夫兼执法官，毕生向来诙谐豁达、酷爱谐趣玩笑，特于此争议山丘之阳，道出余生平最后一则绝妙幽默，但祈其不致在阴冷潮湿之泥土中埋没过久，以致来日重见天日引爆之时，沦为哑火之爆竹。此乃余之压轴绝唱，但愿闻者皆绝倒捧腹、涕泪横流。余，杰里迈亚·吉伯蒂，乃遭余之第二任续弦妻子克莱门蒂娜蓄意毒杀身亡，其乃水手拉尔夫·鲍德布里奇斯与极北外邦蛮妇所育之孽胎。犯下此等滔天罪行，彼得益于其奸夫克里斯托弗·帕格沃克之倾力襄助，该厮乃自封草药行家之外邦无赖。凭肌肤各处奇痒难忍之万般煎熬（此刻作书犹受其苦）以及舌苔密布之黑斑，余心知肚明自己已身中民间深谙医理者口中所谓之夺命毒莓。彼等将毒莓熬煮浓缩，伪饰成桑葚果冻之貌，由余亲爱之发妻亲手奉上，余懵懂无知食之殆尽。余谨奉劝启获此书之人，速速缉拿寻觅一名唤作彼得·皮斯之幼童，乃某酗酒补锅匠之子嗣。盖因该幼童腹饥嘴馋、贪图小钱，仅于一小时前尚挎满一篮此等绝命之毒莓前来兜售，询余可愿出价收买。余为试探虚实，反诘彼莫非以为余之果园罹患虫灾、竟落魄至需向外采买果实之境地？彼供称，见常驻吉伯蒂家宅之那位先生（克里斯托弗·帕格沃克）仅于一周前尚在亲手采摘，料定余家必嗜此物。倘若克里斯托弗·帕格沃克潜逃离境，则请官府画影图形，缉拿一名矮胖粗短、生有胡桃褐色发丝、鼻如雀卵布满斑点、且一只眼为棕色、另一只眼为蔚蓝之异相男子。为使余生平最后这则谐谑之作无懈可击，余强忍心中剧痛，将自该幼童手中购得之部分浆果，投喂给余之马车夫之女、名唤玛乔丽·比奇之小女童所豢养之白兔。余之所以选她，盖因其年方七岁（7岁）且体魄健硕，当来日有人掘出此埋骨幽默之时，极有可能尚存活于此人间山丘之阳。彼若尚在人世，定然历历难忘昔日其心爱白兔如何剧痒抓搔自身、其舌苔如何如毒蛇般黑斑密布，以及最终如何僵毙暴亡之惨状。

===Original===

And I humbly beg her pardon for having played such a cruel trick on a little maid, and I ask my heirs (if so be any of them still alive) to send her a fine buck rabbit, a ham, and ten gold pieces. And, though I am lawman and could put them under arrest before I die, yet, for a time, I hold my hand. Partly because I have been a hunter all my life, and as the hare and deer are given their chance to escape, so shall they have theirs; and partly because I should like to be very far on my wanderings down the Milky Way before Clementina mounts Duke Aubrey’s wooden horse; because I think the sound of her strangling would hurt my ears; and, last of all, because I am very weary. And here I sign my name for the last time.
> Jeremiah Gibberty.

===Chinese===

> 余谨就向那稚嫩小女童施加此等残忍恶作剧，致以最诚挚卑微之歉意；并勒令余之后嗣（倘有幸存之人），务必酬赠彼一头健硕雄兔、一整只肥硕火腿，以及金币十枚（10枚金币）。余虽身兼执法官之职、本可于溘逝前立将彼等双双缉拿归案，然余终究按捺未发。半因余毕生皆为山林猎手，狡兔与麋鹿尚有一线逃生之机，余亦乐见彼等亡命奔逃；半因余深盼在克莱门蒂娜骑上奥布里公爵之木马受缢之前，余早已在漫步银河之幽径上远走高飞，盖因彼窒息气绝之哀鸣恐将刺痛余之双耳；最后，盖因余实已心力交瘁、疲惫不堪。余于此绝笔画押，自此永别。
> 杰里迈亚·吉伯蒂。

## XXI The Old Goatherd / XXI 老放羊翁
===Original===

For a few seconds they stood petrified, and then Luke was seized with panic, and, calling to the little boys to stay where they were, dashed off in pursuit.
Up the path he pounded, from time to time shouting angrily to Ranulph to come back, but the distance between them grew ever wider.
Luke’s ears began to sing and his brain to turn to fire, and he seemed to lose all sense of reality﻿—it was not on the earth that he was running, but through the airless deserts of space.
He could not have said how long he struggled on, for he who runs hard leaves time behind as well as space. But finally his strength gave way, and he fell, breathless and exhausted, to the ground.
When he had sufficiently recovered to think of starting again the diminishing speck that had been Ranulph had completely vanished.
Poor Luke began to swear﻿—at both Ranulph and himself.
Just then he heard a tinkle of bells, and down the bridle-path came a herd of goats and a very ancient herdsman﻿—to judge, at least, from his bowed walk, for his face was hidden by a hood.
When he had got up to Luke, he stood still, leaning heavily on his stick, and peered down at him from underneath the overhanging flap of his hood with a pair of very bright eyes.
“You’ve been running hard, young master, by the looks of ye,” he said, in a quavering voice. “You be the second young fellow as what I’ve seen running this morning.”
“The second?” cried Luke eagerly. “Was the other a little lad of about twelve years old with red hair, in a green leathern jerkin embroidered in gold?”
“Well, his hair was red and no mistake, though as to the jerkin﻿ ﻿…” And here he was seized with a violent attack of coughing, and it took all Luke’s patience not to grab him by the shoulders and shake the words out of him.
“Though as to the jerkin﻿—my eyes not being as sharp as they once were﻿ ﻿…”
“Oh! never mind about the jerkin,” cried Luke. “Did you stop and speak to him?”
“But about that jerkin﻿—you do cut an old man short, you do﻿ ﻿… it might have been green, but then again it might have been yellow. But the young gentleman what I saw was not the one as *you’re* after.”
“How do you know?”
“Why, because he was the Seneschal’s son﻿—the one *I* saw,” said the old man proudly, as if the fact put him at once into a superior position to Luke.
“But it’s the Seneschal’s son﻿—Master Ranulph Chanticleer, that I’m after, too!” cried Luke, eagerly.

===Chinese===

有那么几秒钟，众人惊骇得如泥塑木雕般呆立当场，紧接着卢克陷入了一阵恐慌，他急忙冲那些小男孩高声招呼让他们老老实实待在原地，自己拔腿便向前方狂追而去。
他顺着崎岖的山道一路狂奔，不时怒气冲冲地大声喝喊拉努尔夫快快回来，然而两人之间的距离却反倒越拉越远。
卢克的双耳开始嗡嗡作响，脑袋像着了火一般发烫，他仿佛丧失了一切关于现实的知觉——他似乎不是在坚实的大地上奔跑，而是在虚无缺氧的太虚荒漠中挣扎飞奔。
究竟咬牙苦撑了多久他连自己也说不上来，因为奋力狂奔的人早已将时间与空间一并甩在了身后。然而他的体力终究难以为继，终于气喘吁吁、精疲力竭地瘫倒在地上。
等他缓过一口气来想要重新爬起来追赶时，先前那颗代表拉努尔夫的越来越微小的黑点，早已彻底从视野中消失得无影无踪。
可怜的卢克忍不住破口大骂起来——既咒骂拉努尔夫，也咒骂自己窝囊。
就在这时，他听见了一阵清脆的铃铛声，只见从马道上方缓缓走下来一小群山羊，身后跟着一个极其年迈苍老的放羊人——至少从他那佝偻蹒跚的步态来看如此，因为他的面孔全遮蔽在一顶兜帽之下。
老羊倌走到卢克身旁停下脚步，身子沉沉倚靠在拐杖上，从兜帽低垂的宽边下，用一双格外精明亮堂的眼睛居高临下地打量着他。
“小哥，看你这副架势，刚才跑得可够拼命的啊，”他用颤巍巍的苍老嗓音说道，“你可是我今儿大清早瞧见的第二个（第2个）一路狂奔的年轻小伙子了。”
“第二个？”卢克急切地脱口喊道，“另一个是不是一个年方十二岁（12岁）上下、留着一头红头发、身穿一件金线刺绣绿皮坎肩的小男孩？”
“哎呀，头发是通红的这绝错不了，至于那坎肩嘛……”说到此处，老头忽然剧烈地咳嗽起来，卢克简直要把所有的耐性都耗光了，才强忍住没冲上去抓住他的肩膀逼他吐出话来。
“不过至于那坎肩嘛——我的老花眼早不比当年那般好使了……”
“哎呀！坎肩管它什么颜色呢，”卢克焦急地嚷道，“你到底有没有停下来同他说过话？”
“不过关于那坎肩——你这后生怎么老爱打断老人家的话头呢，你呀……它可能是绿的，不过也可能是黄的。但我见到的那位小少爷，可不是‘你要找’的那一位。”
“你怎么知道的？”
“嗨，因为‘我’瞧见的那一位，可是大总管的亲公子啊，”老头神气活现、满怀自豪地说道，仿佛这一事实刹那间让他在卢克面前占据了高人一等的尊贵地位。
“可我要找的，也正是大总管的公子——拉努尔夫·尚特克利尔少爷啊！”卢克急不可耐地高呼道。

===Original===

“How long is it since you saw him? I *must* catch up with him.”
“You’ll not do that, on your two feet,” said the goatherd calmly. “That young gentleman, and his yellow jerkin and his red hair, must be well on the way to Moongrass by now.”
“To Moongrass?” And Luke stared at him in amazement.
“Aye, to Moongrass, where the cheeses come from. You see it was this way. I’m goatherd to the Lud yeomanry what the Seneschal has sent to watch the border to keep out you know what. And who should come running into their camp about half an hour ago with his red jerkin and his green hair but your young gentleman. ‘Halt!’ cries the Yeoman on guard. ‘Let me pass. I’m young Master Chanticleer,’ cries he. ‘And where are you bound for?’ cries the Yeoman on guard. ‘For Fairyland,’ says he. And then didn’t they all laugh! And the little chap flew into quite a rage, and said he was off to Fairyland, and no one should stop him. And, of course, that just made them laugh all the more. But though they wouldn’t let him go to Fairyland, the young rascal﻿ ﻿…” And here the old man was seized with a paroxysm of wheezy laughter which brought on another bout of coughing.
“Well, as I was saying,” he went on, when he had recovered, “they wouldn’t let him through to Fairyland, but they said they would ride back with him where he came from. ‘No, you won’t,’ says he; ‘my dad,’ says he, ‘don’t want me to go back there, never any more.’ And he whisks out a letter signed by the Seneschal, bidding him leave the widow Gibberty’s farm, where he was staying, and go straight off to Farmer Jellygreen’s at Moongrass. So one of the Yeomen saddled his horse, and the youngster got up behind him, and they set off for Moongrass by one of the cattle-paths running northeast, which comes out at about the middle of the road between Swan and Moongrass. So that’s that, my young fellow.” In his relief Luke tossed his cap into the air.
“The young rascal!” he cried joyfully; “fancy his never having told me he’d got a letter from his Worship, and me expecting that letter for the last three days, and getting stomachache with worry at its not coming! And saying he was off to a certain place, too! A nice fright he’s given me. But thank’ee, gaffer, thank’ee kindly.

===Chinese===

“你看见他有多久了？我‘非得’追上他不可！”
“光凭你这两条腿，可甭想追得上喽，”老放羊翁神色自若地慢条斯理道，“那位小少爷，连同他的黄坎肩和红头发，这当口怕是早就快马加鞭赶去月草的半道上了。”
“去月草？”卢克惊愕万分地瞪大了眼睛。
“对啊，就是出产名贵奶酪的那个月草嘛。事情是这么回事。我是路德义勇卫队的放羊人，就是大总管派来把守边陲、防范那见不得光玩意的卫队。你猜怎么着，约莫半小时前，你那位小少爷挺着他的红坎肩和绿头发（老头嘴快说岔了），冷不防一头撞进了卫队的营地。‘站住！’值宿的卫兵厉声喝道。‘放我过去，我是尚特克利尔家的小少爷！’他嚷嚷道。‘你打算往哪儿去啊？’值宿卫兵又问。‘去仙境之地！’他回答说。哎哟，大伙儿一听全笑翻了天！那个小家伙顿时气得七窍生烟，嚷嚷着他偏要去仙境之地，谁也甭想拦住他。这一来，当然惹得大伙儿笑得更欢了。不过虽说他们不肯由着他去仙境之地，那个小滑头……”说到这里，老头又忍不住发出一阵哮喘般的咯咯怪笑，随之又引来了一阵剧烈的干咳。
“好啦，正如我刚才所言，”等他平复过来后继续说道，“卫兵们不放他去仙境之地，却提出要骑马把他护送回他先前来的地方。‘没门儿！’他说，‘我爹’他说，‘吩咐过我再也别回那鬼地方去了。’说着他猛地从怀里掏出一封盖有大总管印鉴的手令，命他立刻离开投宿的吉伯蒂寡妇的农庄，径直赶往月草的农夫杰利格林家。于是乎，一名卫兵备好马鞍，把小少爷拉上后鞍座，俩人顺着一条往东北延伸的牧牛小道奔月草去了，那条小道正好插在天鹅镇和月草之间的大路当中。事情的经过就是这么着，年轻的小伙子。”
听闻此言，如释重负的卢克高兴得一把将帽子抛向了半空。
“这个小滑头！”他欣喜若狂地喊道，“亏他还瞒着我收到大人的来信，害得我一连等了三天（3天）那封信，愁得胃痉挛都要犯了！居然还嚷着要去那个见鬼的地方！可把我吓得够呛。多谢您了，老人家，真是万分感谢您！

===Original===

And here’s something for you to drink the health of Master Ranulph Chanticleer,” and with a heart as light as a bird’s, he began to retrace his steps down the valley.
But what was that faint sound behind him? It sounded suspiciously like the *Ho, ho, hoh!* of that impudent Willy Wisp, who for a short time, had been one of his Worship’s grooms.
He stopped, and looked round. No one was visible except the old goatherd in the distance, leaning on his stick. What he had heard could have been nothing but the distant tinkle of the goat bells.
When he reached the farm, he found it in a tumult. The little boys had frightened Hazel out of her wits, and confirmed her worst fears by the news that “Master Ranulph had run away towards the hills, and that Master Hempen had run after him.”
“Granny!” cried Hazel, wringing her hands, “a messenger must be sent off posthaste to the Seneschal!”
“Stuff and nonsense!” cried the widow, angrily. “You mind your own business, miss! Long before any messenger could reach Lud, the lads will be back safe and sound. Towards the hills, indeed! That Luke Hempen is a regular old woman. It’s just a bit of Master Ranulph’s fun. He’s hiding behind a tree, and will jump out on them with a ‘Boo!’ Never in my life have I heard so much fuss about nothing.” And then, turning to the farm-servants, who were clustering round the children with scared, excited eyes, she bade them go about their business, and let her hear no more nonsense.
Her words sounded like good sense, but, for all that, they did not convince Hazel. Her deep distrust of the widow was almost as old as herself, and her instinct had told her for some time that the widow was hostile to Ranulph.
Never for a moment did Hazel forget that she, not the widow, was the rightful owner of the farm. Should she for once assert her position, and, in direct defiance of the widow, report what had happened to the lawman of the district and send a messenger to Master Nathaniel?
But, as everybody knows, legal rights can be but weaklings﻿—puny little child princes, cowed by their bastard uncles, Precedent and Seniority.
No, she must wait till she was of age, or married, or﻿ ﻿… was there *any* change of condition that could alter her relations with the widow, and destroy the parasite growth of sullen docility which, for as long as she could remember, had rotted her volition and warped her actions?

===Chinese===

这点零钱留给您买酒喝，权当替拉努尔夫·尚特克利尔少爷祝贺健康吧，”说着，他怀着宛如飞鸟般轻快的欢快心情，拔腿顺着山谷原路折返。
然而，身后隐隐传来的一阵细微声响又是何物？那动静听起来极像那个曾在市长府上当过马夫的刁顽小厮鬼火威利狂妄的‘嚯，嚯，嚯！’冷笑声。
他驻足回首张望。视线所及空空如也，唯有远处那个倚着拐杖的老放羊翁孑然而立。方才所听到的，想必不过是远方山羊颈铃的虚幻回响罢了。
及至回到庄园，只见满院早已乱作一团。那帮小男孩早已将黑兹尔吓得魂飞魄散，向她坐实了最可怕的梦魇：“拉努尔夫少爷朝着群山的方向逃走了，亨彭小哥在后头死命追他呢！”
“奶奶！”黑兹尔痛心疾首地捶胸顿足，“必须十万火急打发信使去给大总管报信！”
“简直胡说八道！”寡妇怒发冲冠地喝斥道，“少多管闲事，死丫头！等信使还没摸到路德城门，那帮小子早就平平安安自己走回来了。朝山里逃？荒谬透顶！那个卢克·亨彭根本是个胆小如鼠的窝囊老娘们。这不过是拉努尔夫少爷在闹着玩罢了。他一准是藏在哪棵大树后头，冷不防跳出来冲他们扮个鬼脸喊声‘吓着了吧！’老娘这辈子还从没见过为这么点芝麻大的小事小题大作闹翻天的。”说着，她转向围在孩子们周围一个个眼神惊恐慌乱的长工仆役们，喝令他们立刻滚回各自的岗位干活，休要再让她听见半句疯言疯语。
她的言辞听起来入情入理，但即便如此，却丝毫未能打消黑兹尔的疑虑。她对寡妇根深蒂固的猜忌防备几乎与她自身的年岁一样漫长，而且她的直觉早就在告诫她：寡妇对拉努尔夫心怀叵测。
黑兹尔无时无刻不在牢记：自己，而非眼前这个寡妇，才是这片庄园无可争议的合法继承人与女主人。她是否应当破天荒头一遭挺身确立自身的权威，公然违抗寡妇的意志，将眼前的一切如实呈报给本地区的执法官，并火速派人向纳撒尼尔老爷告急？
然而，尽人皆知，法定权益往往弱不禁风——宛若被“先例”与“尊长”这两个野种叔父吓破了胆的稚嫩小王子一般。
不，她必须耐心捱到自己成年，或者出阁成婚，或者……世间当真有“任何”境遇的逆转，能改变她受制于寡妇的处境，彻底根除那株寄生在她心头、自她记事起便侵蚀其意志并扭曲其举止的隐忍温顺的毒草吗？

===Original===

Hazel clenched her fists and set her teeth﻿ ﻿… *She would assert herself!﻿—she would!*﻿ ﻿… now, at once? Why not give them, say, till noon, to come back? Yes, she would give them till noon.
But before then, a rather shamefaced Luke arrived with his confession that Master Ranulph had made proper fools of them.
“So, Miss Hazel, if you’ll give me a bite of something, and lend me a horse, I’ll go after the young scamp to Moongrass. To think of his giving us the slip like that and never having told me he’d heard from his father! And there was me expecting a letter from his Worship every day, telling us to leave at once, and﻿ ﻿…”
Hazel raised her eyebrows. “You were expecting a letter ordering you to leave us? How was that?”
Luke turned red, and mumbled something inaudible. Hazel stared at him for a few seconds in silence, and then she said quietly, “I’m afraid you were wise if you asked the Seneschal to remove Master Ranulph.”
He gave her a shrewd glance. “Yes﻿ ﻿… I fear this is no place for Master Ranulph. But if you’d excuse me for being so bold, miss, I’d like to give you a word of warning﻿—don’t you trust that Endymion Leer further than you can see him, and don’t you ever let your Granny take you out *fishing*!”
“Thank you, Master Hempen, but I am quite able to look after myself,” said Hazel haughtily. And then an anxious look came into her eyes. “I hope﻿—oh! I hope that you’ll find Master Ranulph safe and sound at Moongrass! It’s all so﻿ ﻿… well, so very strange. That old goatherd, who do you suppose he was? One meets strange people near the Elfin Marches. You’ll let me know if all is well﻿ ﻿… won’t you?”
Luke promised. Hazel’s words had dampened his spirits and brought back all his anxiety, and the fifteen miles to Moongrass, in spite of a good horse, seemed interminable.
Alas! there was no Ranulph at the Jellygreens’ farm; but, to Luke’s bewilderment, it turned out that the farmer had been expecting him, as he had, a few days previously, received a letter from Master Nathaniel, from which it was clear that he imagined his son was already at Moongrass. So there was nothing for Luke but, with a heavy heart, to start off the next morning for Lud, where, as we have seen, he arrived a few hours after Master Nathaniel had left it.

===Chinese===

黑兹尔咬紧牙关，狠狠攥紧了双拳……‘她一定要树立自己的威信！——她非这么做不可！’……现在，立刻就办？为何不给他们一点宽限，比方说，等到正午时分看他们是否归来？对，她就等到正午时分。
然而还未到正午，一脸羞惭赧颜的卢克便折返回来，坦承拉努尔夫少爷把大家结结实实耍弄了一通。
“所以，黑兹尔小姐，要是您能赏我一口饭吃，再借我一匹快马，我这就动身去月草追那个小淘气包。真没想到他竟敢这般瞒天过海摆脱咱们，而且压根没同我提过他收到了老爷的来信！我先前还日日巴望着大人的手信，命咱们即刻收拾行囊启程呢……”
黑兹尔扬起秀眉。“你先前一直在巴望信件命你们立刻离开咱们这里？这究竟是为何？”
卢克脸颊涨得通红，嘴里含糊不清地嘟囔着什么。黑兹尔默不作声地盯着他端详了片刻，随即语调平静地说道：“我想，倘若你当真恳请大总管把拉努尔夫少爷从这里迁走，那是你极其明智的决断。”
卢克机敏地睃了她一眼。“是的……我唯恐此地绝非拉努尔夫少爷久留之所。不过恕小的斗胆冒昧多嘴，小姐，我倒想给您提个醒——那个恩底弥翁·利尔，只要一脱离您的视线，您就万万不可轻信；而且无论如何，您也绝不能由着您奶奶领您去‘钓鱼’！”
“多谢你，亨彭小哥，但我完全有能力照料好自己，”黑兹尔神态矜持地答道。随即她的眼眸中泛起了一抹忧虑之色。“但愿——噢！但愿你能在月草见到拉努尔夫少爷平安无恙！这一切委实太……咳，太离奇怪诞了。那个老放羊翁，你料想他究竟是何方神圣？在精灵边陲近旁，人们总会撞见些奇奇怪怪的异人。若是沿途一切安好……你可一定要捎信告诉我啊！”
卢克满口应承。黑兹尔的这番话如同一盆冷水浇灭了他的兴致，将所有的忧虑重新唤醒；通往月草的那十五英里（15英里）路程，即便跨着一匹良驹，此刻也显得无休无止般漫长。
呜呼！杰利格林一家的农庄里根本不见拉努尔夫的踪影；然而令卢克百思不得其解的是，农夫本人却早就在翘首期盼着孩子的到来，因其在数日前确实收到了纳撒尼尔老爷的一封手书，信中言之凿凿认定他的公子早已先行抵达了月草。于是，万般无奈的卢克唯有怀着沉重如铅的心情，于翌日清晨硬着头皮踏上返回路德的归途——正如我们在前文所见，当他匆匆赶到府邸时，纳撒尼尔老爷已然离城数小时之久了。

## XVI The Widow Gibberty’s Trial / XVI 吉伯蒂寡妇受审
===Original===

The following day, with all the masquerading that the Law delights in, Master Nathaniel was pronounced in the Senate to be dead. His robes of office were taken off him, and they were donned by Master Polydore Vigil, the new Mayor. As for Master Nathaniel﻿—was wrapped in a shroud, laid on a bier and carried to his home by four of the Senators, the populace lining the streets and greeting the mock obsequies with catcalls and shouts of triumph.
But the ceremony over, when Master Ambrose, boiling with indignation at the outrage, came to visit his friend, he found a very cheerful corpse who greeted him with a smack on the back and a cry of “Never say die, Brosie! I’ve something here that should interest you,” and he thrust into his hand an open in-folio.
“What’s this?” asked the bewildered Master Ambrose.
There was a certain solemnity in Master Nathaniel’s voice as he replied, “It’s the Law, Ambrose﻿—the homoeopathic antidote that our forefathers discovered to delusion. Sit down this very minute and read that trial through.”
As Master Ambrose knew well, it was useless trying to talk to Nat about one thing when his mind was filled with another. Besides, his curiosity was aroused, for he had come to realize that Nat’s butterfly whims were sometimes the disguise of shrewd and useful intuitions. So, through force of long habit, growling out a protest about this being no time for tomfoolery and rubbish, he settled down to read the volume at the place where Master Nathaniel had opened it, namely, at the account of the trial of the widow Gibberty for the murder of her husband.
The plaintiff, as we have seen, was a labourer, Diggory Carp by name, who had been in the employ of the late farmer. He said he had been suddenly dismissed by the defendant just after harvest, when it was not easy to find another job.
No reason was given for his dismissal, so Diggory went to the farmer himself, who, he said, had always been a kind and just master, to beg that he might be kept on. The farmer practically admitted that there was no reason for his dismissal except that the mistress had taken a dislike to him. “Women are kittle cattle, Diggory,” he had said, with an apologetic laugh, “and it’s best humouring them. Though it’s hard on the folks they get their knife into. So I fear it will be best for everyone concerned that you should leave my service, Diggory.”

===Chinese===

翌日，伴随着法律所钟爱的全套滑稽假面把戏，纳撒尼尔老爷在元老院被正式宣判为“死亡”。他的官袍被当众褫夺，改由新任市长波吕多洛·维吉尔老爷穿戴上身。至于纳撒尼尔老爷本人——则被严严实实裹上一袭裹尸布，停放在灵柩上，由四位元老抬送回府，街道两旁挤满了围观市民，用刺耳的嘘声与凯旋般的狂呼喝彩夹道迎送这场虚伪的假葬礼。
然而仪式一结束，当安布罗斯老爷因对这桩奇耻大辱义愤填膺而登门探望友人时，却发现眼前伫立着一个快活得异乎寻常的“死尸”，只见他一掌重重拍在安布罗斯背上，热情地招呼道：“千万别轻言放弃，布罗西！我这儿有一件你准定感兴趣的好宝贝，”说着便把一本翻开的对折大部头硬塞进了他手里。
“这是什么？”一头雾水的安布罗斯老爷诧异地问道。
纳撒尼尔老爷的嗓音里带上了一丝少有的庄严肃穆，他答道：“这是法律，安布罗斯——咱们的祖先发掘出的用以对付虚妄幻觉的顺势疗法解药。立刻坐下，把这场审判从头到尾仔细读上一遍。”
正如安布罗斯老爷所深知的那样，当纳特满脑子被某件事情占据时，旁人休想同他探讨其他任何话题。况且他的好奇心也确实被勾了起来，因为他早已领悟到，纳特那些看似如飞蝶般漫不经心的怪诞念头，骨子里往往掩藏着敏锐而切中要害的非凡直觉。于是，出于多年的老习惯，他一边嘴里嘟嘟囔囔抱怨着如今可不是胡闹鬼混的时机，一边在纳撒尼尔老爷翻开的书页前安坐下来，仔细研读起吉伯蒂寡妇被控谋杀亲夫一案的庭审实录。
正如前文所述，原告是一名名叫迪戈里·卡普的长工雇农，曾受雇于已故的老农夫。他陈述说，就在秋收刚过不久，他便被女被告冷不防无故解雇了，而那个时节想要在乡间重新找份差事极为不易。
鉴于解雇时并未给出任何正当由头，迪戈里遂亲自去找老农夫讨个说法——据他称，老主人向来是一位仁厚公正的东家——哀求东家能继续留用他。农夫实际上坦承，解雇他并无任何过错，纯粹是由于女主人看他不顺眼。“女人啊全都是些阴晴不定的难缠牲口，迪戈里，”老农夫带着歉意的苦笑如是说，“最好顺着她们的毛摸。虽说被她们盯上的人日子难熬。所以依我看，为了大伙儿都好，你还是离开我的庄园另谋高就吧，迪戈里。”

===Original===

But he gave him a handful of florins over and above his wages, and told him he might take a sack of lentils from the granary﻿—if he were careful that the mistress did not get wind of it.
Now, Diggory had a shrewd suspicion as to why the defendant wanted to get rid of him. Though she was little more than a girl﻿—she was the farmer’s second wife and more like his daughter’s elder sister than her stepdame﻿—she had the reputation of being as staid and sensible as a woman of forty. But Diggory knew better. He had discovered that she had a lover. One evening he had come on her in the orchard, lying in the arms of a young foreigner, called Christopher Pugwalker, a herbalist, who had first appeared in the neighbourhood just before the great drought.
“And from that time on,” said Diggory, “she had got her knife into me, and everything I did was wrong. And I believe she hadn’t a moment’s peace till she’d got rid of me. Though, if she’d only known, I was no blab, and not one for blaming young blood and a wife half the age of her husband.”
So he and his wife and his children were turned out on the world.
The first night they camped out in a field, and when they had lighted a fire Diggory opened the sack that, with the farmer’s permission, he had taken from the granary, in order that his wife might make them some lentil soup for supper. But lo and behold! instead of lentils the sack contained fruit﻿—fruit that Diggory Carp, as a west countryman, born and bred near the Elfin Marches, recognised at the first glance to be of a kind that he would not dream of touching himself or of allowing his wife and children to touch﻿ ﻿… the sack, in fact, contained fairy fruit. So they buried it in the field, for, as Diggory said, “Though the stuff be poison for men, they do say as how it’s a mighty fine manure for the crops.”
For a week or so they tramped the country, living from hand to mouth. Sometimes Diggory would earn a little by doing odd jobs for the farmers, or by playing the fiddle at village weddings, for Diggory, it would seem, was a noted fiddler.
But with the coming of winter they began to feel the pinch of poverty, and his wife bethought her of the trade of basket-making she had learned in her youth; and, as they were camping at the time at the place where grew the best osiers for the purpose, she determined to see if her fingers had retained their old cunning.

===Chinese===

但他格外体恤地在工钱之外多赏了他一把弗罗林银币，并吩咐他可以从粮仓里自取一袋扁豆带走——前提是必须小心谨慎，切莫让女主人察觉风声。
其实，迪戈里心里对女被告何以急于将他扫地出门早有一番透彻的暗中推测。尽管那妇人当时年纪轻轻宛若少女——她是老农夫续弦的二房，与其说是继母，倒不如说更像农夫亲生女儿的同胞长姐——但在外人眼里，她向来博得沉稳老练、犹如四十岁（40岁）妇人般端庄知礼的美名。然而迪戈里却洞若观火。他早已撞破了她私通奸夫的丑事。某个傍晚，他在果园里撞见她正依偎在一个唤作克里斯托弗·帕格沃克的年轻外邦人怀里，那是个自称草药郎中的外乡客，在大旱年爆发前夕方才首次在这一带村落露面。
“从那一刻起，”迪戈里供述道，“她就对我怀恨在心、处处挑刺，我干什么全都是错的。我相信若是不把我彻底撵走，她一天都休想合眼安枕。其实她若是心里有数，我向来是个守口如瓶的人，绝不是那种爱拿年轻人血气方刚、或者少妻配老夫的闲话搬弄是非之徒。”
于是，他和妻儿老小被无情逐出庄园，流离失所。
颠沛流离的第一夜，他们露宿在荒郊田野里，生起一堆篝火后，迪戈里解开了那只经农夫恩准从粮仓里扛出来的口袋，好让妻子煮上一锅扁豆汤果腹。然而定睛一瞧！口袋里装的根本不是扁豆，而是满满当当的一袋果子——身为生于斯长于斯、紧邻精灵边陲的西部乡民，迪戈里·卡普一眼便辨识出那种果子：那是他自己连碰都不敢碰、更绝不敢由着妻儿染指分毫的邪门之物……那袋子里装的，分明是仙果！于是他们将果子就地深埋在田野之中，正如迪戈里所言：“这玩意儿于凡人而言虽是穿肠毒药，老辈人却传言说拿它作庄稼的肥料那是再肥沃不过的灵丹妙药了。”
随后约莫一周左右，一家人风餐露宿、走村串庄，过着吃了上顿没下顿的日子。偶尔迪戈里靠给沿途农庄打零工挣点薄碎铜板，或者在乡村婚礼上拉小提琴助兴，因为迪戈里似乎是个小有名气的手艺提琴手。
但随着寒冬降临，一家人愈发感到生计艰难、饥寒交迫，他的妻子想起年轻时曾学过编织柳条竹筐的手艺；既然他们当时歇脚露宿的去处恰好生长着最适宜编筐的优质柳条，她便决心瞧瞧自己的手指是否还保留着当年的灵巧手艺。

===Original===

As the sap of these particular osiers was a deadly poison, she would not allow the children to help her to gather them.
So she set to and make wicker urns in which the farmers’ wives could keep their grain in winter, and baskets of fancy shapes for lads to give to their sweethearts to hold their ribbons and fal-lals. The children peddled them about the countryside, and thus they managed to keep the pot boiling.
The following summer, shortly before harvest, Diggory’s eldest girl went to try and sell some baskets in the village of Swan. There she met the defendant, whom she asked to look at her wares, relying on not being recognised as a daughter of Diggory’s, through having been in service at another farm when her father was working at the Gibbertys’.
The defendant seemed pleased with the baskets, bought two or three, and got into talk with the girl about the basket-making industry, in the course of which she learned that the best osiers for the purpose were very poisonous. Finally she asked the girl to bring her a bundle of the osiers in question, as making baskets, she said, would make a pleasant variety, of an evening, from the eternal spinning; and in the course of a few days the girl brought her, as requested, a bundle of the osiers, and was well paid for them.
Not long afterwards came the news that the farmer Gibberty had died suddenly in the night, and with it was wafted the rumour of foul play. There was an old custom in that part of the country that whenever there was a death in the house all the inmates should march in procession past the corpse. It was really a sort of primitive inquest, for it was believed that in the case of foul play the corpse would bleed at the nose as the murderer passed it. This custom, said Diggory, was universally observed in that part of the country, even in cases as free from all suspicion as those of women dying in childbed. And in all the taverns and farmhouses of the neighbourhood it was being whispered that the corpse of the farmer Gibberty, on the defendant’s walking past it, had bled copiously, and when Christopher Pugwalker’s turn had come to pass it, it had bled a second time.
And knowing what he did, Diggory Carp came to feel that it was his duty to lodge an accusation against the widow.

===Chinese===

由于这种特定柳条的汁液蕴含致命剧毒，她严禁孩子们插手帮她采摘采集。
于是她全力开工，编织出供农妇在严冬储粮的大柳条瓮，以及各式造型精巧美观的提篮小筐，供年轻小伙买去赠予心上人存放发带与珠花饰物。孩子们挎着提篮沿街走村串户兜售，全家人借此勉强糊口度日。
第二年盛夏将近收割前夕，迪戈里的大女儿前往天鹅镇村中碰运气兜售筐篮。在那儿她巧遇了女被告，仗着当年父亲在吉伯蒂家做工时自己一直在另一座农场当差、对方绝不可能认出自己是迪戈里的闺女，便上前殷勤招徕女被告过目她的货物。
被告似乎对那些提篮颇为赏识，买下了两三只（2、3只），并同这姑娘闲聊起编筐的行当手艺，其间获悉用于编织的这种上等柳条汁液极具剧毒。末了，被告央求姑娘改日为她捎带一捆这种柳条来，声称入夜后闲来无事编编筐子，也好从日复一日没完没了的纺线活计里换换心情调剂一番；数日之后，姑娘依约送来了一大捆剧毒柳条，并得到了极为丰厚的酬赏。
不久之后，乡野间骤然传来了农夫吉伯蒂在深夜暴毙身亡的噩耗，随之四起的便是遭遇谋害的流言蜚语。在当地乡野有一项古老相传的风俗：每逢宅内有人暴亡，所有同住一屋檐下的人皆须排成长队从尸骸前鱼贯走过。这实际上是一场原始的民间验尸检核，因为乡民深信倘若死者死于非命，当真正的凶手走过遗体身旁时，尸骸的鼻腔便会汩汩淌出鲜血。迪戈里陈述道，这项古训在彼处乡民中向来被奉若神明、无一例外遵守，哪怕是产妇难产身亡这等毫无嫌疑的命案亦概莫能外。而当时在周遭所有的酒馆农庄里，人们皆在交头接耳窃窃私语：当女被告从农夫吉伯蒂的遗体前走过时，尸骸鼻孔中曾大量狂喷鲜血；而轮到克里斯托弗·帕格沃克踱步经过时，尸骸竟又二次淌血！
联想起自身所掌握的桩桩疑点，迪戈里·卡普深感自己有义不容辞的责任出面检举揭发这位恶毒的寡妇。

===Original===

His two reasons, then, for thinking her guilty were that the corpse had bled when she passed it, and that she had bought from his daughter osiers the sap of which was poisonous. The motive for the crime he found in her having a young lover, whom she wished should stand in her dead husband’s shoes. It was useless for the defendant to deny that Pugwalker was her lover﻿—the fact had for months been the scandal of the neighbourhood, and she had finally lost all sense of shame and had actually had him to lodge in the farm for several months before her husband’s death. This was proved beyond a shadow of a doubt by the witnesses summoned by Diggory.
As for the bleeding of the corpse: vulgar superstitions did not fall within the cognizance of the Law, and the widow ignored it in her defence. However, with regard to that other vulgar superstition to which the plaintiff had alluded, fairy fruit, she admitted, in passing, that very much against her wishes her late husband had sometimes used it as manure﻿—though she had never discovered how he procured it.
As to the osiers﻿—she allowed that she had bought a bundle from the plaintiff’s daughter; but that it was for no sinister purpose she was able conclusively to prove. For she summoned various witnesses﻿—among others the midwife from the village, who was always called in in cases of sickness﻿—who had been present during the last hours of the farmer, and who all of them swore that his death had been a painless one. And various physicians, who were summoned as expert witnesses, all maintained that the victim of the poisonous sap of osiers always died in agony.
Then she turned the tables on the plaintiff. She proved that Diggory’s dismissal had been neither sudden nor unjust; for, owing to his thieving propensities, he had often been threatened with it by her late husband, and several of the farm-servants testified to the truth of her words.
As to the handful of florins and the sack of lentils, all she could say was that it was not like the farmer to load a dishonest servant with presents. But nothing had been said about two sacks of corn, a pig, and a valuable hen and her brood, which had disappeared simultaneously with the departure of the plaintiff. Her husband, she said, had been very angry about it, and had wanted to have Diggory pursued and clapped into gaol; but she had persuaded him to be merciful.

===Chinese===

因此，他认定寡妇有罪的核心依据有二：其一，走过尸身前尸体淌血；其二，寡妇曾向其女采买含有致命毒汁的柳条。至于作案动机，则在于她暗通年轻奸夫，处心积虑妄图让奸夫取已故亡夫的家业而代之。被告想要抵赖帕格沃克是其奸夫纯属白费心机——此事数月来早已成为街谈巷议的丑闻，到后来寡妇甚至彻底丧尽廉耻，在丈夫暴毙前数月甚至公然将奸夫接至农庄同住。经迪戈里传唤的数名证人当庭作证，这一铁证确凿无疑。
至于尸体淌血之说：市井荒诞迷信向来不属于法律审理采信的管辖范畴，寡妇在自辩中对此完全嗤之以鼻、不屑一顾。然而，关于原告提及的另一桩市井迷信——亦即仙果一事，她顺水推舟坦承：已故先夫生前确曾不顾她的极力劝阻，偶尔采纳仙果充当庄稼肥料——尽管她至死亦未曾弄清先夫究竟是从何处暗中弄到这等货色的。
至于那捆柳条——她爽快承认确实曾从原告之女手中购得一捆；但她却能无可辩驳地拿出铁证证明那绝非用于任何险恶图谋。盖因她传唤了多名证人——包括每次村中有人罹疾必受邀前去探视的乡村接生婆——他们皆在农夫弥留弥际守候在侧，且人人赌咒发誓证实老农夫临终前神态极其安详、未曾遭受丝毫痛苦折磨。而多位奉召出庭充当法医专家证人的名医，亦一致断言：凡中剧毒柳条汁液者，临终前无一例外皆在撕心裂肺、肠穿肚烂的极度抽搐惨嚎中痛苦毙命。
随即，寡妇施展手段将原告反将一军。她举证证明迪戈里的解雇既非事发仓促，亦非无端构陷；盖因该厮生性手脚不干净、惯爱小偷小摸，生前早被先夫屡次厉声斥责威胁开革，多名庄园仆役当庭证实了她的证词句句属实。
至于所谓随手赏赐的几枚弗罗林银币与那袋扁豆，寡妇反讥道：以先夫的为人作风，绝无可能向一个劣迹斑斑的盗窃家贼馈赠如此重礼。然而原告方却对两袋谷物（2袋谷物）、一头肥猪，以及一只连同全窝雏鸡的名贵母鸡在原告离去之时同时不翼而飞的重大失窃案只字未提。寡妇宣称，当时其夫对此雷霆大怒，执意要飞骑缉拿迪戈里下狱治罪，全赖自己苦苦相劝才饶恕其一条生路。

===Original===

The long and the short of it was that the widow left the court without a stain on her character, and that a ten years’ sentence for theft was passed on Diggory.
As for Christopher Pugwalker, he had disappeared shortly before the trial, and the widow denied all knowledge of his whereabouts.

===Chinese===

这场官司的最终结局是：寡妇一身清白、毫无瑕疵地昂首走出了公堂，而迪戈里却因盗窃之罪被当庭重判十年徒刑（10年徒刑）。
至于克里斯托弗·帕格沃克，早在开庭受审前夕便已潜逃得无影无踪，而寡妇则全盘推托否认对其下落行踪有半分知情。

## VI The Wind in the Crabapple Blossoms / VI 海棠花丛中的风
===Original===

About a week later, Mistress Hempen received the following letter from Luke:

===Chinese===

大约一周后，亨彭太太收到了卢克寄来的如下手信：

===Original===

> Dear Auntie﻿—I trust this finds you as well as it leaves me. I’m remembering what you said, and trying to look after the little master, but this is a queer place and no mistake, and I’d liefer we were both safe back in Lud. Not that I’ve any complaint to make as to victuals and lodging, and I’m sure they treat Master Ranulph as if he was a king﻿—wax candles and linen sheets and everything that he gets at home. And I must say I’ve not seen him looking so well, nor so happy for many a long day. But the widow woman she’s a rum customer and no mistake, and wonderful fond of fishing, for a female. She and the doctor are out all night sometimes together after trout, but never a trout do we see on the table. And sometimes she looks so queerly at Master Ranulph that it fairly makes my flesh creep. And there’s no love lost between her and her granddaughter, her step-granddaughter I *should* say, her who’s called Miss Hazel, and they say as what by the old farmer’s will the farm belongs to her and not to the widow. And she’s a stuck-up young miss, very high and keeping herself to herself. But I’m glad she’s in the house all the same, for she’s well liked by all the folk on the farm and I’d take my oath that though she’s high she’s straight. And there’s a daft old man that they call Portunus and it’s more like having a tame magpie in the house than a human man, for he can’t talk a word of sense, it’s all scraps of rhyme, and he’s always up to mischief. He’s a weaver and as cracked as Mother Tibbs, though he do play the fiddle beautiful. And it’s my belief the widow walks in fear of her life for that old man, though why she should beats me to know. For the old fellow’s harmless enough, though a bit spiteful at times. He sometimes pinches the maids till their arms are as many colours as a mackerel’s back. And he seems sweet on Miss Hazel though she can’t abear him, though when I ask her about him she snaps my head off and tells me to mind my own business. And I’m afraid the folk on the farm must think me a bit high myself through me minding what you told me and keeping myself *to* myself. Because it’s my belief if I’d been a bit more friendly at the beginning (such as it’s my nature to be) I’d have found out a thing or two. And that cracked old weaver seems quite smitten by an old stone statue in the orchard.

===Chinese===

> 亲爱的姑婆——但愿您老人家收到此信时身体安康，恰如我在此地一切安好。我牢记着您的叮嘱，竭力照看好小少爷，但这地方委实透着邪门，我宁愿咱们俩都平平安安早日回到路德。倒不是说在吃住上有什么可挑剔的，我敢说他们待拉努尔夫少爷简直像待国王一样——点白蜡烛，睡细麻布床单，家里能享用的一切这儿样样齐全。而且我不得不承认，好长日子以来我还从没见他身子骨这么硬朗、心里这么痛快过呢。但是那个寡妇妇人可委实是个难缠古怪的厉害角色，作为一个女流之辈，还出奇地嗜好钓鱼。有时她和那位医生整夜整夜在外面钓鳟鱼，可饭桌上却连半条鳟鱼的影子都瞧不见。有时她看拉努尔夫少爷的眼神说不出的古怪，瞧得我汗毛倒竖、脊梁骨直发凉。而且她和她孙女——确切说是她继孙女，也就是那位黑兹尔小姐之间，那是连半分情分也没有，大伙儿都说按照老农夫的遗嘱，这庄园本该归她而不是归那寡妇。那姑娘是个心高气傲的娇小姐，冷冰冰的独来独往。不过我还是庆幸宅子里有她在，因为庄园上的雇工长工全都很敬重她，我敢赌咒发誓，她人虽孤傲，心术却正得很。宅子里还有一个唤作波尔图努斯的疯癫老头，家里养着这么个人简直就像养了一只驯服的喜鹊，嘴里吐不出半句正经人话，尽是些颠三倒四的打油诗碎片，整天净捣乱。他是个织布匠，疯疯癫癫得跟蒂布斯大妈一个德行，不过小提琴拉得委实漂亮。我心里琢磨着，寡妇为了提防那老头简直成天提心吊胆、怕得要命，可到底为了什么我却百思不得其解。因为老头本身倒也算人畜无害，无非偶尔有点促狭恶毒罢了。他有时冷不防使劲拧女仆的胳膊，掐得人家胳膊青一块紫一块宛如鲭鱼的脊背。他似乎对黑兹尔小姐挺殷勤讨好，小姐却恨透了他，每次我向小姐打听他，她就劈头盖脸冲我发作，叫我少管闲事。我唯恐庄园上的雇工们也觉得我为人高傲孤僻，因为我时刻记着您的吩咐，不同他们过多搭腔、独善其身。依我看，要是开头我能随和亲切一点（那本就是我的天性），说不定早就摸清一两桩内情了。还有，那个疯疯癫癫的老织工似乎对果园里的一尊古老石像格外着迷。

===Original===

He’s always cutting capers in front of it, and pulling faces at it, like a clown at the fair. But the widow’s scared of him, as sure as my name’s Luke Hempen. And Master Ranulph does talk so queer about him﻿—things as I wouldn’t demean myself to write to an old lady. And I’d be very glad, auntie, if you’d ask his Worship to send for us back, because I don’t like this place, and that’s a fact, and not so much as a sprig of fennel do they put above their doors.—And I am, Your dutiful grandnephew,
> Luke Hempen.

===Chinese===

> 他成天在那石像跟前上蹿下跳、挤眉弄眼，宛如集市上的滑稽小丑。但寡妇打心眼底里害怕他，就像我唤作卢克·亨彭一样确凿无疑。而且拉努尔夫少爷谈起他时言辞古怪至极——那些疯话我委实羞于下笔向一位老夫人禀报。姑婆，要是您能求求老爷下令把咱们接回去，我可就谢天谢地了，因为我打心底里讨厌这地方，千真万确，而且他们家门楣上连一小枝茴香草都没挂。——您孝顺的侄孙，
> 卢克·亨彭。

===Original===

Hempie read it through with many a frown and shake of her head, and with an occasional snort of contempt; as, for instance, where Luke intimated that the widow’s linen sheets were as fine as the Chanticleers’.
Then she sat for a few minutes in deep thought.
“No, no,” she finally said to herself, “my boy’s well and happy and that’s more than he was in Lud, these last few months. What must be must be, and it’s never any use worrying Master Nat.”
So she did not show Master Nathaniel Luke Hempen’s letter.
As for Master Nathaniel, he was enchanted by the accounts he received from Endymion Leer of the improvement both in Ranulph’s health and state of mind. Ranulph himself too wrote little letters saying how happy he was and how anxious to stay on at the farm. It was evident that, to use the words of Endymion Leer, he was learning to live life to a different tune.
And then Endymion Leer returned to Lud and confirmed what he had said in his letters by his accounts of how well and happy Ranulph was in the life of a farm.
The summer was simmering comfortably by, in its usual sleepy way, in the streets and gardens of Lud-in-the-Mist. The wives of Senators and burgesses were busy in still-room and kitchen making cordials and jams; in the evening the streets were lively with chattering voices and the sounds of music, and ’prentices danced with their masters’ daughters in the public square, or outside taverns, till the grey twilight began to turn black. The Senators yawned their way through each other’s speeches, and made their own as short as possible that they might hurry off to whip the Dapple for trout or play at bowls on the Guild Hall’s beautiful velvety green. And when one of their ships brought in a particularly choice cargo of rare wine or exotic sweetmeats they invited their friends to supper, and washed down the dainties with the good old jokes.
Mumchance looked glum, and would sometimes frighten his wife by gloomy forebodings; but he had learned that it was no use trying to arouse the Mayor and the Senate.
Master Nathaniel was missing Ranulph very much; but as he continued to get highly satisfactory reports of his health he felt that it would be selfish not to let him stay on, at any rate till the summer was over.
Then the trees, after their long silence, began to talk again, in yellow and red. And the days began to shrink under one’s very eyes.

===Chinese===

亨皮紧皱眉头、连连摇头读完了整封信，间或还不屑地从鼻子里哼出一声轻蔑的冷笑；例如读到卢克声称寡妇家的细麻布床单同尚特克利尔家的一样考究体面时。
随即她陷入了沉思默想。
“不，不，”最后她暗自嘀咕道，“我的宝贝孙儿如今身子健旺、心里快活，这比在路德最后几个月的模样强出太多了。该来的挡不住，多拿烦心事打扰纳特老爷向来毫无用处。”
于是她并未将卢克·亨彭的信呈给纳撒尼尔老爷过目。
至于纳撒尼尔老爷，从恩底弥翁·利尔寄来的书信中获悉拉努尔夫的体魄与精神状况皆大有起色，他感到心旷神怡。拉努尔夫自己也寄回了几封字迹稚嫩的小信，诉说自己在这儿是何等快乐，又是何等渴望能继续留在农庄。显而易见，借用恩底弥翁·利尔的话来说，孩子正在学着以一种截然不同的欢快调子去过活了。
随后，恩底弥翁·利尔回到了路德，当面证实了他在信中所言：拉努尔夫在乡间农庄的生活中是何等健康快活。
夏日的时光在雾中路德的街巷与花园间悠然流淌，一如既往地惬意而带着几分倦怠。元老与市民们的贵妇们在蒸馏室和后厨里忙得不可开交，熬制着甜露与果酱；一到傍晚，街市上便回荡着欢声笑语与悠扬乐声，学徒工们在市政广场或酒馆门前同东家千金们翩翩起舞，直到灰蒙蒙的暮色彻底凝为墨黑。元老们在彼此冗长乏味的演说中哈欠连天，各自的发言也尽可能言简意赅，好飞奔赶去达普尔河上甩竿钓鳟鱼，或是去公会大厅那宛若天鹅绒般平整葱翠的草坪上玩草地滚球。而每当哪一家的货船捎回了一批极其上等的珍稀名酒或异域甜点，他们便盛情相邀宾朋小聚，就着用陈年醇厚的老笑话将珍馐佳酿一饮而尽。
芒姆钱斯面色阴郁，偶尔用悲观可怖的不祥预感吓唬妻子；但他早已看透，想要唤醒市长与元老院纯属白费心机。
纳撒尼尔老爷心中格外思念拉努尔夫；但既然源源不断接获孩子健康状况大有起色的喜讯，他便觉得倘若不放任孩子在乡间多待一阵（至少等夏天过完），未免显得自己太过自私狭隘。
不久，在经历了漫长的缄默之后，树木重新用金黄与赤红的华彩开口倾吐。而白昼也开始在人们眼皮子底下肉眼可见地迅速缩短。

===Original===

And Master Nathaniel’s pleached alley was growing yellower and yellower, and on the days when a thick white mist came rolling up from the Dapple it would be the only object in his garden that was not blurred and dimmed, and would look like a pair of gigantic golden compasses with which a demiurge is measuring chaos.
It was then that things began to happen; moreover, they began at the least likely place in the whole of Lud-in-the-Mist﻿—Miss Primrose Crabapple’s Academy for young ladies.
Miss Primrose Crabapple had for some twenty years “finished” the daughters of the leading citizens; teaching them to sing, to dance, to play the spinet and the harp, to preserve and candy fruit, to wash gauzes and lace, to bone chickens without cutting the back, to model groups of still life in every imaginable plastic material, edible and non-edible﻿—wax, butter, sugar﻿—and to embroider in at least a hundred different stitches﻿—preparing them, in fact, to be one day useful and accomplished wives.
When Dame Marigold Chanticleer and her contemporaries had first been pupils at the Academy, Miss Primrose had only been a young assistant governess, very sentimental and affected, and full of nonsensical ideas. But nonsensical ideas and great practical gifts are sometimes found side by side, and sentimentality is a quality that rarely has the slightest influence on action.
Anyhow, the ridiculous gushing assistant managed bit by bit to get the whole direction of the establishment into her own hands, while the old dame to whom the school belonged became as plastic to her will as were butter, sugar or wax to her clever fingers; and when the old lady died she left her the school.
It was an old rambling redbrick house with a large pleasant garden, and stood a little back from the high road, about half a mile beyond the west gate of Lud-in-the-Mist.
The Academy represented to the ladies of Lud all that they knew of romance. They remembered the jokes they had laughed at within its walls, the secrets they had exchanged walking up and down its pleached alleys, far more vividly than anything that had afterwards happened to them.
Do not for a moment imagine that they were sentimental about it. The ladies of Lud were never sentimental. It was as an old comic song that they remembered their schooldays. Perhaps it is always with a touch of wistfulness that we remember old comic songs.

===Chinese===

纳撒尼尔老爷花园里的绿廊隧道变得愈发金黄耀眼，每逢达普尔河翻滚起浓密的白茫茫大雾时，那条长廊便成了满园之中唯一未被雾气吞没消融之景，远远望去宛若创世巨神用来丈量洪荒混沌的一柄硕大无朋的黄金圆规。
正是在那个当口，种种变故开始接踵而至；而且，其开端偏偏始于整座雾中路德最不可思议的所在——普丽姆罗丝·克拉巴普小姐的女子名媛学园。
在整整二十年（20年）间，普丽姆罗丝·克拉巴普小姐专事为显贵望族的千金闺秀进行终身修养的“精修”，悉心传授她们声乐、舞蹈、弹奏击弦古钢琴与竖琴、腌渍蜜饯鲜果、淘洗薄纱蕾丝、剔除整鸡骨架而不划破后背皮肤、用世间一切可食用或不可食用的各色可塑材料（蜡、牛油、糖霜）捏塑静物雕塑，以及刺绣至少一百种（100种）互不重样的精妙针法——简言之，正是把她们培养调教为来日贤良淑德、多才多艺的名门贵妇。
当年玛丽戈尔德·尚特克利尔夫人与其闺中密友在学园求学之际，普丽姆罗丝小姐还不过是个年轻的助理女教师，生性多愁善感、矫揉造作，满脑子都是离经叛道的荒诞念头。然而荒诞不经的怪想有时竟能与卓越非凡的务实才能并存不悖，而伤春悲秋的情愫也极少能对实际行动产生半分阻碍牵制。
不论如何，这位浮夸多姿的滑稽助教施展手腕，一步步将整个学园的统领大权尽收掌心，而学园原本的那位老校长，在她那双灵巧手指的摆弄之下，变得如同牛油、糖霜或石膏蜡一般对其百依百顺；当老太太溘然长逝时，便顺理成章将整座学园悉数遗赠予她。
学园是一座带有宽敞宜人后花园的古老红砖宅邸，坐落在大道稍向内退缩之处，位于雾中路德西门外约半英里处。
在这座名媛学园之中，凝聚着路德城中所有贵妇所能参悟体会的全部浪漫传奇。相较于日后在她们漫长人生中所经历的一切风浪，她们对当年在学园四壁之内捧腹绝倒的笑话、在并肩漫步绿廊隧道时推心置腹交换的体己私房话，印象反而要鲜活深刻得多。
诸位千万莫以为她们对此怀有何等多愁善感的伤感情怀。路德城的夫人们骨子里绝不感伤。她们是将其视作一首滑稽逗趣的老流行歌谣，来追忆各自的闺阁韶华的。或许在追忆陈年幽默小调之时，我们心头往往总免不了泛起那么一丝怅惘与依恋吧。

===Original===

It was at any rate as near as the ladies of Lud could get to the poetry of the past. And whenever Dame Marigold Chanticleer and Dame Dreamsweet Vigil and the rest of the old pupils of the Academy foregathered to eat syllabub and marzipan and exchange new stitches for their samplers, they would be sure sooner or later to start bandying memories about these funny old days and the ridiculous doings of Miss Primrose Crabapple.
“Oh, *do* you remember,” Dame Marigold would cry, “how she wanted to start what she called a ‘Mother’s Day,’ when we were all to dress up in white and green, and pretend to be lilies standing on our mothers’ graves?”
“Oh, yes!” Dame Dreamsweet would gurgle, “And mother was so angry when she found out about it. ‘How dare the ghoulish creature bury me alive like this?’ she used to say.”
And then they would laugh till the tears ran down their cheeks.
Each generation had its own jokes and its own secrets; but they were always on the same pattern; just as when one of the china cups got broken, it was replaced by another exactly like it, with the same painted border of squills and ivy.
There were squills and ivy all over the Academy, embroidered on the curtains in each bedroom, and on all the cushions and screens, painted in a frieze around the wall of the parlour, and even stamped on the pats of butter. For one of Miss Primrose Crabapple’s follies was a romantic passion for Duke Aubrey﻿—a passion similar to that cherished by highchurch spinsters of the last century for the memory of Charles I. Over her bed hung a little reproduction in watercolours of his portrait in the Guildhall. And on the anniversary of his fall, which was kept in Dorimare as a holiday, she always appeared in deep mourning.
She knew perfectly well that she was an object of ridicule to her pupils and their mothers. But her manner to them was not a whit less gushing in consequence; for she was much too practical to allow her feelings to interfere with her bread and butter.
However, on the occasions when her temper got the better of her prudence she would show them clearly her contempt for their pedigree, sneering at them as commercial upstarts and interlopers. She seemed to forget that she herself was only the daughter of a Lud grocer, and at times to imagine that the Crabapples had belonged to the vanished aristocracy.

===Chinese===

无论如何，这已是路德城的贵妇名媛们所能触碰到的昔日往昔诗意的极限所在。每当玛丽戈尔德·尚特克利尔夫人与甜梦·维吉尔夫人，以及学园的昔日同窗聚首一堂品尝杏仁糖膏、甜奶油混合冻饮并切磋绣样的新颖针法时，兜兜转转最终总免不了拿那些荒唐可笑的旧日时光，以及普丽姆罗丝·克拉巴普小姐当年的滑稽行径打趣调侃。
“噢，你还记得吗，”玛丽戈尔德夫人会咯咯娇笑起来，“她当年居然异想天开想要创立什么‘母亲节’，让咱们大伙儿全都换上绿白相间的衣裙，假扮成伫立在各自生母坟茔上的百合花？”
“哎哟可不是嘛！”甜梦夫人笑得直喘气，“我母亲知道之后简直气炸了肺，直嚷嚷：‘那个丧门星老怪物怎敢这样把我活埋了？’”
于是大家笑得前仰后合、眼泪直淌。
每一代弟子都有属于她们各自的笑话与秘密；但模式却始终如出一辙；正如一只瓷杯被打碎了，便会换上一只一模一样的新杯子，杯口边缘同样印着由海葱与常春藤构成的花边。
在学园里，海葱与常春藤的花纹随处可见，刺绣在每间卧房的窗帘上、靠垫与屏风上，绘制在客厅墙面环绕的腰线饰带上，甚至压印在一块块待客黄油上。盖因普丽姆罗丝·克拉巴普小姐平生最大的疯癫痴念，便是对奥布里公爵怀有一股浪漫狂热的崇拜——恰似上世纪高教会派的老姑娘对查理一世的深情缅怀一般。在她的床头，悬挂着公会大厅里公爵画像的水彩微型复制品。而在多里梅尔定为公假休市的公爵被逐纪念日，她向来必定通体缟素、深沉服丧以示哀悼。
她心里明镜似的，知道自己在弟子们及其母辈眼中不过是个天大的笑柄。然而在面对她们时，她的做作谄媚之态却未因此收敛半分；盖因她生性极为务实精明，绝不容许私人情感砸了自己的饭碗营生。
